All labels and descriptions come from Messages_*.properties (Messages.getInfo, resolved in the viewer's locale with English fallback; the key itself when missing).

4.2.2 Synchronise with an external application (ConnectorSyncUserService)

  • Set-up (setupObjectContext, runs on every request): calls ConnectorsInstaller.ensure, then loads ConnectorRepository.mappingsFor(dataspace, dataset, tablePath) for the current table. That list contains active, non-child mappings that apply to the table; a mapping with an empty dataspace applies everywhere the dataset exists. If loading throws, the exception message is shown as an error.
  • Presets:
  • mapping: read from the session input parameters (getInputParameterValue(false, "mapping")), else from the request. It becomes the initial selection. It can only select among the mappings of this table, so an invalid value cannot trigger another mapping.
  • inputs: same lookup. Stored as run-time inputs and shown as the banner Run-time inputs of this run: {0}.
  • dryRun: applied once per service instance. The value false unticks the simulation box.
  • Form bean ftConnectorSyncOptions:
Field Type Values / facets Default
mapping string, required enumeration: id → <label> [<connector> / <operation>, <direction>] preset, else last choice, else first mapping
scope string, required SELECTED Selected records · VIEW Displayed records (current filter) · ALL Whole table · UNSENT Not sent yet (outbound) · CHANGED Changed since the last send, failures to retry, deletions (outbound) · FAILED Failed sends to retry (outbound) · DATASPACE_DELTA Created, modified or deleted since the initial snapshot (outbound) SELECTED
maxRecords int, required 1…500 (ABSOLUTE_MAX = 500) 100
dryRun boolean, required "Simulate only (nothing is written)" true
  • Buttons: Run (ftcsRun) and Close (no confirmation on leave).
  • Run behaviour: 1. Looks up the chosen mapping by id among the table's mappings. If none is found: Choose a mapping. (error). 2. Feed mapping (it has a keyPredicate): computes ConnectorService.runtimeInputs(mapping, requestedInputs, ignored), then calls importFrom(session, repo, mapping, inputs, max, dryRun, "userService"). The scope is ignored. Ignored input names produce the message Run-time input(s) ignored because the mapping does not read them as param.name: {0}. 3. Scope UNSENT, CHANGED, FAILED or DATASPACE_DELTA: beginRun, then sendScope(session, repo, mapping, table, null, OutboundScope.parse(scope), max, dryRun, "userService"), with message Run {0} (replay its failures with POST /replay/{0}), then endRun. 4. Otherwise: the request is getAllRecords (ALL), getFilteredRecords (VIEW) or getSelectedRecords (SELECTED). A null request falls back to all records. Calls syncRecords(session, request, mapping, max, dryRun, "userService"). An empty result gives No record in the chosen scope.. 5. Any exception: The synchronisation failed: {0} (error).
  • Display:
  • If the table has no mapping: No active mapping is declared for {0}: add one in the Connectors dataset (Mapping table).
  • Before a run: Choose a mapping and click Run. Simulation shows the changes without writing them.
  • After a run, the summary line is {0} record(s): {1} updated, {2} unchanged, {3} without data, {4} skipped, {5} error(s)., followed by Simulation: nothing was written. when dry-run. Below it, a table: Record | Status (CSS colour per status) | Changes (list of changes and errors) | Message (… (<ms> ms)).
  • Run-time inputs format: name=value pairs separated by ; or line breaks. An entry is kept only when it has = and the feed mapping reads ${param.<name>}; every other entry is reported as ignored (ConnectorService.runtimeInputs).

4.2.3 Import an OpenAPI specification (OpenApiImportUserService, OpenApiImport)

  • The form is plain HTML, read back from the request:
Field (request name) Label
ftoaId Connector id (letters and digits; default: from the title) — placeholder RechercheEntreprises
ftoaLabel Label (default: the specification title)
ftoaUrl Specification URL — placeholder https://.../openapi.json
ftoaSpec (textarea) ...or the specification text
ftoaReplace (checkbox) Replace an existing connector of the same id (its operations and fields; secrets are kept)
ftoaDryRun (checkbox) Preview only, create nothing
  • Hint: Give a URL to the specification, or paste it (JSON or YAML). The connector is created with type rest; secrets stay to be entered on the connector.
  • Buttons: Import (ftoaRun) and Close.
  • Import behaviour: 1. If a URL is given, OpenApiImport.fetch(url) downloads it: HttpJsonClient with a 10 s connect timeout, a 30 s read timeout, user agent EBX-Connectors/1.0 (openapi) and Accept: application/json, application/yaml, text/yaml, */*. A non-2xx response raises OpenAPI: GET <url> answered HTTP <status>; an I/O failure raises OpenAPI: cannot download <url>: …. 2. OpenApiImport.run(session, repo, id, label, text, replace, dryRun) parses the spec with OpenApiImporter.parse/build (core). It produces a draft: id, label, baseUrl, authType, apiKeyHeader, description, properties, operations, notes. 3. With dry-run, the report is returned and nothing is written. 4. Otherwise ConnectorsInstaller.ensure runs first. A missing dataset raises The Connectors dataset is not available. An existing connector without replace raises Connector '<id>' already exists: choose another id or allow replacement. 5. The procedure runs with all privileges. It writes id, label, type=rest, baseUrl, authType, apiKeyHeader, description, active=true.
    • On creation, apiKey is set to TO_BE_SET.
    • On replacement, the existing row is modified (secrets untouched) and every property whose key starts with op. or auth. is deleted; other properties are kept.
    • Then one ConnectorProperty is created per draft property.
    • A failure raises Import failed: ….
    • ConnectorRegistry.get().invalidate() runs afterwards.
  • Report:
  • Title: Preview of connector {0} (nothing created). / Connector {0} created. / Connector {0} updated.
  • Summary: Base URL {0}, authentication {1}, {2} operation(s), {3} propert(y/ies).
  • Then the list of operations and the notes.
  • Errors are shown in red with the raw exception message.

4.2.4 Manage connector conflicts (ConflictManagerUserService, three declarations)

  • Scope is resolved on first display:
  • Dataspace selection → dataspace = branch name.
  • Dataset selection → dataset = dataset name.
  • Table selection → table = table path. If the table is /root/Conflict itself, the scope is cleared and the selection designates conflicts instead: selected records of the view, or the opened record. conflictIds = those ids, or null when nothing is selected. Scope label: All open conflicts / Selected conflict(s).
  • Record selection (not on the Conflict table) → recordKey = the record's primary key.
  • Parameters dataset, table and recordKey narrow the scope further. They are read from the request, else from the workflow interaction input parameters.
  • inWorkflow = session.isInWorkflowInteraction(false).
  • List: ConflictResolver.list("OPEN", dataspace, dataset, table, recordKey), restricted to conflictIds when set. It is re-read on every request.
  • Form bean ftConflictOptions: comment (string, max 500 characters). Label Comment; help Stored on every conflict decided in this round.; the default is the last comment entered.
  • Screen:
  • Title Connector conflicts - {scope}, then Scope: {0} - {1} open conflict(s). The scope text is dataspace / dataset / table / key / #id-or-count, or *.
  • With no conflict: No open conflict in this scope.
  • Otherwise a hint, links (Select all: take / keep / later, client-side radio selection), the comment row, and a table with columns:
    • Record: record label, plus table / key.
    • Field: the path without ./.
    • Current value: value, the connector that wrote it or manual entry, and a trust badge.
    • Proposed value: value, proposing connector, trust badge.
    • Detected: yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm.
    • Decision: radios Take the proposed value / Keep the current value / Decide later (default).
  • A trust badge is green when higher than the other side and orange when lower. A missing current trust counts as 100; a missing proposed trust counts as 0.
  • Buttons:
  • Apply the decisions (ftcfApply): each conflict follows its radio (ftcfDecision_<id>). take → resolver.accept(session, conflict, comment); keep → resolver.reject(...); later or none → skipped.
  • Take all proposed values (ftcfAcceptAll) forces take on every listed conflict. Keep all current values (ftcfRejectAll) forces keep.
  • After any of the three: a log line per decided conflict (<record> - <outcome message>, in red when it failed), then {0} accepted, {1} rejected, {2} failed; {3} still open. (error style when something failed). The resolved counter accumulates across rounds.
  • Workflow only — Complete the task (ftcfComplete): sets the interaction outputs resolvedCount (the accumulated count) and remainingCount (open conflicts now), calls interaction.complete(outputs) and closes. A failure shows The task could not be completed: {0}.
  • Outside a workflow: Close.

4.2.5 Delete a mapping and everything attached (DeleteMappingUserService)

  • Selection: the record, or the distinct selected records of the table view. On later requests every row is looked up again and deleted rows are dropped.
  • Title: Delete {0} mapping(s) and everything attached.
  • With no selection: No mapping selected: open a mapping, or select rows in the Mapping table. (only a Close button).
  • Preview table, one row per mapping, built from ConfigTransfer.attachments(dataset, id). Counts are recursive over child mappings:
Column Meaning
Mapping id - label
Rules rules deleted
Inputs inputs deleted
Sub-object mappings child mappings deleted
Outbound states outbound states deleted
Provenance rows (unlinked) provenance rows that lose their reference
Conflicts (unlinked) conflict rows that lose their reference
  • Intro: The following elements will be deleted with each mapping; provenance and conflict rows are kept and only lose their reference to the mapping.
  • Hint: The synchronisation log is kept as is. This cannot be undone: export the configuration first if you may need the mapping again.
  • Confirmation button Delete {0} mapping(s) (ftdmConfirm) calls ConfigTransfer.deleteMapping(session, repo, id) for each mapping (§3.9).
  • Success: {0} mapping(s) deleted, {1} attached row(s) removed. The screen then shows only Close.
  • Otherwise: Deletion failed: [<label>: <error>, ...].
  • Note: attachments() does not count SynchronizationCheckpoint rows, although cascade() deletes them.

4.2.6 Design the mapping (MappingDesignerUserService + mapping-designer.js)

Server state. The service keeps: direction and responseRoot (initialised from the record), sampleKey, posted rules and inputs, external fields and their origin (catalog or sample), a message, and a test result.

Form bean ftMappingDesignerOptions: sampleKey (string). Label Sample record (primary key); help A record of the target table used to load a sample document and to test the rules. Empty: the first record.

Hidden fields submitted with every button: ftmdRules (JSON array of {id, source, target, transformation, policy, comment}), ftmdDirection (INBOUND/OUTBOUND), ftmdResponseRoot, ftmdInputs (JSON array of {name, expression}), ftmdChildCollection, ftmdChildTable, ftmdChildLink, ftmdChildKeys.

readPosted (runs before each action): - Clears the message and the test result. - Keeps the posted rules and inputs when non-empty. - A direction change clears the cached external fields. - Takes responseRoot as posted (trimmed) and reads sampleKey from the bean.

Buttons (left bar, in order), then Close:

Button (id) Label Behaviour
ftmdSave Save the rules One procedure, in the user's session (no privilege escalation). Details below.
ftmdTest Test on the sample (simulation) Details below.
ftmdSample Load the external fields Details below.
ftmdInvert Create the reverse mapping Details below.
ftmdChild Create a sub-object mapping Details below.
ftmdResponse Create the response mapping Details below.

Save the rules (ftmdSave): 1. Updates the mapping's direction and responseRoot (empty → null). 2. Rules are upserted in posted order with positions 10, 20…. A posted rule whose id matches an existing MappingRule.id of this mapping updates that row; any other rule creates a row. Every rule is written with active=true, policy (invalid → IF_EMPTY), an empty source or transformation written as null, and its comment. 3. Existing rules not kept are deleted. 4. If inputs were posted, every MappingInput of the mapping is deleted and recreated from the posted list (entries with a blank name or expression are dropped). 5. Result: {0} rule(s) saved., or Saving failed: {0} / The rules could not be read: {0}. After a save, rules and inputs are re-read from the database.

Test on the sample (ftmdTest): - Builds a fresh MappingDefinition from the record: current direction, responseRoot, stored inputs, posted rules. - Picks the sample record: the given key, else the first record of the target table found through DatasetLocator. - Runs ConnectorService.syncRecord(session, record, definition, dryRun=true, "designer"). - Message Test on {0}: {1} - {2} (error style when the status is ERROR), then a result block Simulation on {0} listing changes and errors, or the message alone. - No sample record: No sample record found in the target table. - Failure: The test failed: {0}.

Load the external fields (ftmdSample): - Uses the root mapping of the parent chain (its connector, operation, inputs). - Inputs are resolved against the sample record with Expressions.resolve. Posted inputs win over stored ones. - Calls connector.describeFields(ctx, operation, inputs) to get the catalogue. - OUTBOUND: the catalogue is merged with MappingEngine.buildOutbound(...) flattened for the sample record (up to MAX_SAMPLE_FIELDS = 400), plus every rule target. Messages: Sample loaded from {0}: {1} field(s) with their values., or No sample record found… when there is no record. - INBOUND without a record: the catalogue alone. - INBOUND with a record: connector.execute(...). For a sub-object mapping the document is narrowed with narrowToCollection: root responseRoot, then for each chain link the first element of collectionPath, then that link's responseRoot. An empty document gives The connector returned nothing for the sample record (inputs {0}); the catalog is shown instead. (error). Otherwise the flattened document is merged into the catalogue (values become samples). - Failure: Loading the sample failed: {0}.

Create the reverse mapping (ftmdInvert): - Creates a new mapping: same connector, operation, dataspace, dataset and table; opposite direction; label <label> (<direction lower-case>); active=false. - Copies the inputs. - For each current rule with a non-empty source, creates a swapped rule (source↔target), policy IF_EMPTY, no transformation. Constants have no inverse and are skipped. - Message: Reverse mapping #{0} created ({1}), inactive: open it to adjust the operation and the inputs.

Create a sub-object mapping (ftmdChild): - Requires the collection, child table and link field: Collection, child table and link field are required. - Creates a mapping with the same connector, operation, dataspace and dataset; the current direction; label <label> / <collection>; tablePath = child table; parent = this id; collectionPath, parentLinkField, childKeyFields (null if blank); active=true. - Message: Sub-object mapping #{0} created for {1} -> {2}: open it to design its rules.

Create the response mapping (ftmdResponse): - OUTBOUND only; otherwise A response mapping only makes sense for an outbound mapping (EBX -> service); an inbound mapping already stores the answer. - If an INBOUND non-child mapping with the same connector, operation, table and dataset exists: The response mapping already exists: #{0} {1}. - Otherwise creates it: INBOUND, label <label> / response, same target, responseRoot = current, active=true. Message Response mapping #{0} created: open it to choose which elements of the answer are stored in EBX.

Page model ftmdModel (JSON written with addJS_cr, then the script connect/mapping-designer.js?v=<minute stamp> is appended): - direction, responseRoot, connector, connectorLogo, operation, operationKind, operationDescription, table, externalOrigin. - isChild, collectionPath, parentLinkField, children[] (id, label, collectionPath, tablePath, rules). - inputs[]: required inputs of the operation first, then optional ones, then stored inputs not declared by the operation. Each carries expression and required. - sourceObjects[] / sourceDiscoveryError: only for type postgresql with operation query. Built with discoverObjects(firstPage("", 500)); only readable objects; each has query = SELECT * FROM "<schema>"."<object>" LIMIT 100. - responseMapping (the twin, or null); tables[] (every table path of the target dataset); labels[] (about 90 designer bundle keys); connectorError. - externalFields[] (path, label, type, sample). When nothing is cached, the connector catalogue is read with constant inputs only (inputs without ${); OUTBOUND adds the rule targets. - ebxFields[]: terminal fields of the target table. Association and computed fields are skipped; groups are recursed. Each carries path (./a/b), label, type (fk <table> for foreign keys, list of … for multi-occurrence fields), group, pk, groupPath. - rules[], transformations[]: 28 built-in hints plus any other Transformations.names(). - If the target table cannot be read: Table <path> cannot be read in dataset <ds> (<dataspace>): <msg> (error).

Built-in transformation hints: trim, upper, lower, capitalize, left(n), right(n), substring(start,end), replace(a,b), regex(pattern,group), digits, prefix(p), suffix(s), default(v), constant(v), nullIfEmpty, nullIf(v), isoDate, isoDateTime, date(inFormat,outFormat), map(A=B;C=D;*=default), join(', '), first, count, boolean, number, round(n), fk(/root/Table), lookup(/root/Table,./field,./result).

Page behaviour (mapping-designer.js)

Tool bar - Direction select <connector> → EBX / EBX → <connector>. Changing it with existing links asks to confirm (Changing the direction clears the links of this mapping…) and clears the rules. - Response root field. - + Constant value: arms constant mode; the next click on an output field creates a rule with constant(). - Unsaved-changes marker; Hide unmapped fields; rule count. - Diagram / Table view toggle. - Zoom 60–180 % in 10 % steps (buttons or Ctrl/⌘+wheel). - Full screen (Esc leaves it).

Service call section (collapsible) - Hint: inbound — what is sent; outbound — call parameters, the document is the body. - The PostgreSQL source picker replaces the sql input with the object's query (keeping its |> pipeline) and clicks Load the external fields automatically. - One row per input: expression text, an Insert an EBX field… select (inserts ${./path}), removable unless required, and a transformation-step chain. - An input expression's pipeline follows the last |>. - + Add a parameter prompts for a name.

Panes - Input (left) and Output (right). Inbound: left = connector document, right = EBX table. Outbound: the reverse. - Hierarchical trees: JSON paths split on .; EBX paths split on / with group labels. - Type badges: LIST, FK, OBJ, NUM, DT, DATE, BOOL, STR. - Grey sample value, primary-key marker, per-pane search, expand/collapse all, N mapped / M counter.

Links - Drag an input port onto an output row, or click an input field then an output field. - Dropping on an already mapped output re-points that rule, because there is one rule per output field. - A rule with a transformation, or a constant, is drawn as a node in the middle canvas (auto-layout, collisions pushed down). A node's output port can be dragged to re-target the rule. - Clicking an output field that has a rule selects that rule.

Inspector (selected link) - Source chip → editable step chips (arguments inline, ✕ to remove, Add a step… select) → target chip. - Constant value (edits the argument of constant()), raw pipeline text, policy select (IF_EMPTY / ALWAYS / TRUSTED), comment, Delete the rule.

Table view: # | Source | Transformation | Target | Policy | Comment.

Outbound only — response section: the twin card, or No response mapping yet….

Sub-object section - Child cards and a form: collection, child table select, link field, key fields. - Clicking an array row of the document side fills the collection with name[*].

Every change serialises rules, inputs, direction and root into the hidden fields. Nothing is saved until Save the rules is clicked.

4.2.7 Connector catalog (ConnectorCatalogUserService)

Data - Records = the filtered table-view selection, so EBX quick search, criteria, saved views and permissions apply. The total count is getAllRecords. - Sorted by label, case-insensitive. - Categories come from ConnectorCategory, ordered by displayOrder (missing order = 999). - A connector whose category is empty or unknown goes to a synthetic uncategorized group: Other / Connectors without a recognized catalog category.

Toolbar - Search box: Search connectors, descriptions or types. - Advanced EBX search link to the native table view. - Count: {0} connectors or {0} of {1} connectors. - Category chips: All plus one per category.

Card per connector - Logo from logoUrl, falling back to default.svg (also on image load error). - Readiness badge from ConnectorStatusEvaluator.evaluate: INACTIVE → Inactive (card greyed out); UNAVAILABLE → Unavailable; MISCONFIGURED, unreadable configuration or exception → Needs setup; otherwise Ready. - Connection badge from ConnectorConnectionStateService.status: Connected / Reconnect (STALE) / Connection failed / Not connected (also used on exception). - Category label, type (Unknown type when empty), label (fallback: id, then Unnamed connector), description (clamped to 3 lines), id. - Each card is evaluated independently, so one failing connector never hides the others.

Link target - /ebx-ui/ui/advanced/data/<dataspace key>/<dataset>/root/Connector?workspaceDisplay=record:./id='<id>', opened in _top. - Clicking a card stores {id, url} in sessionStorage key ftccRecordOrigin, with the catalog URL ...?service=ConnectorCatalog&serviceMode=TABLE_VIEW. - ConnectorForm later uses that entry to send the record's native Close back to the catalog. - The catalog clears the key when it renders.

Client scripts - If the top window path contains /ui/custom/<perspective>/, every /ui/advanced/data/ link and return URL is rewritten to /ui/custom/<perspective>/data/. - Search and chip filtering hide cards and empty groups; No connectors match your search. shows when nothing matches.

4.2.8 Connector diagnostics (ConnectorDiagnosticsUserService, ConnectorDiagnosticsAdapter)

Access - Readable means the session's permission on the record is not hidden. - When not readable: Access denied / This connector record is not readable in your EBX session. and no action buttons.

Automatic action on open: request parameter ftcdAction = test | connect | preview | disconnect. This is how the connector form's buttons launch it.

Buttons: Validate configuration (ftcdValidate), Test connection (ftcdTest), Preview capabilities (ftcdPreview), Disconnect (ftcdDisconnect), Close. - Each button re-checks readability before acting. - The close callback is a plain native close. It never redirects to /ebx-ui, which would nest a workspace inside the manager bridge.

Actions (all headlines and details are passed through Redaction.redact):

Action Behaviour Headline
validate ConnectorStatusEvaluator.evaluate(config); details = each validation issue, else Status: <status> Configuration is valid / Configuration needs attention; Connector not found
test ConnectorRegistry.connector(id) (descriptor validation, secret resolution, egress allow-list), then connector.testConnection(ctx); details: implementation label, message, elapsed ms or The connector accepted its configuration, but does not provide a live connectivity probe yet. Connection test passed / Connection test failed / Connection test is not supported / Connector test failed
preview Details: category and authentication methods. BoundedInspectionRuntime.discover(firstPage("", 25)): lists label (id) — type; for the first readable object, describe (Schema: N field(s)) and preview limited to 5 records (Read-only sample: N record(s), B byte(s) (truncated) plus each record as JSON). If discovery is unsupported: the descriptor operations and the discovery message. <n> discoverable object(s); Capability preview unavailable
connect ConnectorConnectionStateService.verifyAndRecord(connector, ctx, config). If the status is not CONNECTED: Connection verification failed. Otherwise preview, with details prefixed by Connection verification was recorded; no database socket is retained. Connected and database objects discovered / Connected; discovery needs attention
disconnect ConnectorConnectionStateService.disconnect(config) Connector disconnected — Stored verification was revoked; no external database session was retained.
  • Intro text: Safe connector diagnostics / Validate the saved configuration, test connectivity, or preview a bounded read operation when the connector can do so safely. Diagnostics never invoke write operations and do not modify EBX or external data.
  • Note: connect and disconnect do write the connection* fields of the Connector row.

4.2.9 Connector operations (ConnectorOperationsUserService)

  • Read-only dashboard over getFilteredRecords() of the SyncLog table view, so EBX permissions and filters apply.
  • KPI tiles: visible calls (row count), connectors (distinct connector values), failures (status ERROR).
  • One card per connector (a missing connector is Unknown):
  • {n} successful (any status other than ERROR or SKIPPED; a missing status is UNKNOWN, which counts as successful), {n} failed, {n} skipped.
  • Average duration: <sum durationMs / total> ms, or —.
  • Only a Close button.

4.2.10 Release notes (ReleaseNotesUserService)

Sort and content - Releases sorted by releaseDate descending, then version descending (string comparison). Rows with status DRAFT are skipped. - Header: kicker EBX Connectors, heading Connect data to EBX with confidence, intro, and Export as PDF button. - ftrnExportPdf() clones the page, opens every <details>, removes the button, writes the clone to a new window with the stylesheet, and calls print() after 500 ms. - What you can do section: four cards (Set up a connection; Choose and map data; Test before you run; Run and monitor a flow) and a note.

Per release - Version label v<version>. Versions starting with development-preview show Development preview and the title Using EBX Connectors. - Status badge: releaseNotes.status.<STATUS>, else the raw value with _ replaced by spaces. - Date and summary. - Technical release details (collapsed) holds the metrics. - Items are grouped by type, with headings Available to help you work with data (FEATURE), Improvements you may notice (FIX), Security and safeguards, Before you update (UPGRADE), Before you begin (KNOWN_ISSUE). The detail is shown only when it differs from the title. - Delivery history for administrators (collapsed): one <details> per pass, open unless COMPLETE. Each shows label, status badge, a <progress> bar, remaining gates and steps (status, label, detail). - Footer Build {0} · Commit {1} when either value is set. - No published release: No published release notes are available yet.

Packaged content (release-notes.xml, 4 development releases, no formal release):

id Content
development-preview-2026-09-09-pass10 Metrics: catalog offerings 54, connector types 57, declared operations 234, passes 1–8 checklist 70/73, complete passes 6/10. Passes 1–10 (progress 100/80/100/100/100/80/100/95/80/100) with steps. New / improved / known-issue items.
development-preview-2026-09-12-catalog-and-data-fixes 8 fixes (catalog view error, perspective kept, flow edit/design sign-out, categories, EBX logo, JDE/Oracle Fusion logos, branding overflow), 1 improvement, 1 known issue (closing a flow may leave the panel loading).
development-preview-2026-09-13-openlineage-and-ebx-catalog openlineage sink type, EBX catalogue operations, per-run lineage, credential guide, run ids; known issue: a new sink is seen within 30 s.
development-preview-2026-09-13-declared-lineage Declared lineage, POST /lineage/declare + MCP tool, broker dataset naming; known issue: changing a connector's base URL does not re-describe its mappings.

4.3 UI components (record forms, widgets, functions)

Perspective colours (audit C-40). Every pane of the module (the user services of §4.2 and the record forms below) first writes ThemeTokens: a <style data-ebx-theme>:root{…}</style> block read from the perspective that displays it (UserServiceRequest.getCurrentPerspective().getTheme(), UIFormContext.getCurrentPerspective().getTheme()). Tokens and their PerspectiveTheme getter: --ebx-main (getMainColor), --ebx-focus (getFocusColor), --ebx-header (getHeaderColor), --ebx-second-header (getSecondHeaderColor), --ebx-button (getButtonsHighlightColor), --ebx-table-header (getTableHeaderBackgroundColor), --ebx-table-odd / --ebx-table-even (getTableOdd/EvenBackgroundColor), --ebx-table-border (getTableBodyInnerHorizontalBordersColor, else getTableBodyOuterBorderColor), --ebx-selection (getPerspectiveNavigationPaneSelectionColor); --ebx-main-text, --ebx-header-text, --ebx-button-text are the theme's own text colour (getMainTextIconColor, getHeaderTextColor, getButtonsHighlightTextColor) when it reaches 4.5:1 on its background, else white, #202124 or black. Module tokens derived from the main colour: --ftc-accent-soft (9 % tint), --ftc-accent-line (25 % tint), --ftc-accent-ink (main colour darkened until 4.5:1 on white and on the soft tint). Only #rgb, #rrggbb, rgb()/rgba() and CSS named colours are written; anything else, an empty colour, no theme or no perspective writes no token. The stylesheets and style constants read var(--ebx-…, <previous colour>), so a screen without tokens looks as before. Semantic colours (success, error, warning, information blue), neutral greys, the connector logos and the perspective menu icons (SVG files) stay fixed. The release notes' print window copies the block. ThemeColourScanTest fails when an accent colour of the module appears outside a var() fallback or a pane does not write the tokens.

4.3.1 Connector record form (ConnectorForm, declared recordForm on /root/Connector)

  • The form uses native EBX rows and widgets throughout, so validation, permissions, encrypted fields and Save/Revert keep working.
  • Stylesheet connect/connector-form.css?v=4.
  • Every pane injects the contextual return script: when sessionStorage.ftccRecordOrigin holds this record's id and a same-origin /ebx-ui/ URL, the last button of .ebx_ActionsBack (native Close) is intercepted and navigates the top window back to that URL. A MutationObserver waits for the bar to appear.

Home tab (overview)

Area Content
Connector identity — "Name, catalog placement and implementation" rows id, label, description, category, type, active (only those present in the model)
Integration summary Connector ID (or Assigned on save); Direction coverage = count of Mapping rows with this connector (No flows configured / {0} configured flow(s)); Configuration = count of ConnectorProperty rows (Standard defaults / {0} runtime properties)
Catalog branding — "Logo used across connector experiences" rows logo, uploadedLogo

Tab "Connection & configuration" - Endpoint & credentials (main column): - PostgreSQL setup block, type postgresql only: JDBC URL example jdbc:postgresql://host:5432/database, schema property hint, readiness chips. - Endpoint ready when the jndi property is set or the URL starts with jdbc:postgresql://. - Identity ready when jndi is set or a username is present. - Neutral chip: PostgreSQL JDBC driver is packaged with the module. - Credential fields and guide (§3.2). - Connection actions (existing record only; otherwise Save the connector before testing or connecting.). The buttons open ConnectorDiagnostics in the manager bridge (/ebx/) with ftcdAction: - Test connection. - Connect (label Connect & discover for PostgreSQL). - Disconnect, only when the state is not NOT_CONNECTED. - Discover schemas & tables (preview), PostgreSQL only. - A state chip Not connected / Connected / Verification stale / Connection failed, plus Verified {date}, and the help Uses the saved connector settings; save changes before testing. - Aside: - The three credential guide panels. - Runtime policy: rows connectTimeoutMs, readTimeoutMs, trust. - Validate & test: Open connector diagnostics button, or Save the connector before running diagnostics. - Credential security banner: Password and API token fields use EBX encrypted password widgets and remain governed by record permissions. - Egress posture (existing record only; Egress.posture(config) is recomputed on every render): - COVERED: This connector's host is covered by the current ebx.connectors.allowedHosts list. - NOT_ENFORCED: Outbound calls are not restricted to an allow-list. An administrator must set ebx.connectors.allowedHosts before this connector goes to production. - NOT_COVERED: This connector's host is not in the current allow-list. It will be refused the next time it runs — an administrator needs to add it below. - For the last two, it also shows Current source: <Egress.source()> (or the example -Debx.connectors.allowedHosts=file:/etc/ebx/connectors-allowed-hosts.txt (one host per line, # comments, no restart needed to edit it)) and Add to it: <Egress.example(config)>. - Connector properties: - When a descriptor is known, a "Configuration expected by this connector" list from DescriptorConfiguration.visibleProperties(descriptor, values). The values include authentication and authType. Each entry shows label, type, and the flags required / encrypted-secret / advanced / configured, or default {0} (never for secrets), plus the description. - If no property applies: No additional properties apply to the current authentication mode. - Then the native ./properties association widget, or Save the connector before adding properties. in creation.

Tab "Data flows" - Help and Create or manage a data flow note. - In creation: Save the connector before adding related records. - Otherwise: - A New data flow block with a Create mapping button: the native Create service on /root/Mapping, through the manager bridge preview. - One card per Mapping row with this connector: direction badge (in/out), Active/Inactive badge, label (or primary key), operation, dataspace/dataset/table (— when empty), and buttons. - Card buttons navigate the top window (never an iframe preview). Under a /ui/custom/<perspective>/ path the route is rewritten to that perspective: - Edit flow → /ebx-ui/ui/advanced/data/<dataspace key>/<dataset>/root/Mapping?workspaceDisplay=record:./id=<id> (id unquoted, it is an int). - Design & test → the same URL plus &service=MappingDesigner&serviceMode=RECORD. - Run flow (only when the flow's dataspace, dataset and table are all set) → /ebx-ui/ui/advanced/data/B<flow dataspace>/<dataset><table>?service=ebx-connectors@ConnectorSync&serviceMode=TABLE_VIEW&serviceParams=<base64 {"mapping":"<id>"}>. - Mapping id resolution: Mapping/id, else the occurrence key, else repo.mappingFor(...). - Hint: Activation is controlled by the Active setting in Edit flow. - Empty: No data flows are configured yet. Choose Create mapping above to add the first one.

4.3.2 Credential guide (ConnectorCredentialsPane + connector-credentials.js)

Inputs - Offered modes = CredentialGuide.modesFor(descriptor). - Current mode = stored authType. When empty or unknown: NONE if it is offered or nothing is offered, else the first offered mode. - OAuth flow = CredentialGuide.normalizeFlow(property oauth.flow). Default client_credentials.

Main column - Type strip: descriptor label + credentials.type.<type>.hint. - Mode cards, one per AuthType (NONE, BASIC, API_KEY_HEADER, BEARER, OAUTH2): name + short text. Modes not offered are disabled with title Not offered by this connector type. - Description of the current mode. - Native rows baseUrl, authType, username, password, apiKeyHeader, apiKey, each with row id ftcc_<field> inside #ftcc-rows. - Secret length meter (#ftcc-meter). - Per offered mode, an Expected properties panel from CredentialGuide.properties(type, mode, flow). Each property shows its state (required/optional/derived/unused) and a value: - Derived value when not configured. - configured for secret names (containing secret, password, privatekey, refreshtoken, or token but not tokenurl). - The stored value otherwise. - The default flow for oauth.flow. - Hint: descriptor description, else credentials.type.<type>.prop.<name>, else credentials.prop.<name>. - A property counts as set when it is not blank and not TO_BE_SET. - Readiness strip (#ftcc-ready) and legend.

Aside (three panels, one pane per offered mode) - What to enter: numbered steps from credentials.type.<type>.<MODE>.step.N.title/text, else credentials.mode.<MODE>.step.N.* (N < 20). For OAUTH2 with a non-default flow, credentials.flow.<flow>.step.N.* are appended. A warning comes from ...type.<type>.<MODE>.warn, else ...mode.<MODE>.warn, formatted level|message with level warn / crit / good. With no steps: Nothing to enter: no credential is sent with this mode. - Where to get it: credentials.type.<type>.<MODE>.obtain.N, else credentials.type.<type>.obtain.N, plus .note. Otherwise Nothing to obtain. (NONE) or See the provider's documentation for this connector type. - How the module authenticates: send text and code sample from type.<type>.<MODE>.send/code, else the flow code, else the mode text. Placeholders resolved from the record: - {apiKeyHeader} (default X-API-Key) - {baseUrl} (default https://…) - {clientId} - {tenantId} - {oauth.tokenUrl} (the property, else the derived value with tenantId/baseUrl substituted, else https://…/oauth2/token) - Text inside `backticks` is rendered as code.

Bundle coverage (English) - Modes NONE, BASIC, API_KEY_HEADER, BEARER (warning: permanent tokens only; the field takes 255 characters), OAUTH2. - Flows password, refresh_token, jwt_bearer. - Field labels: apiKey per mode (API key / Token / Client secret / Secret read by the connector); username for OAUTH2 = OAuth user. - Property hints: oauth.flow, oauth.tokenUrl, clientId, oauth.scope, oauth.refreshToken, oauth.privateKey, tenantId, jndi, security.protocol, sasl.mechanism, apiVersion. - Type-specific guides: powerbi (OAUTH2 steps and obtain list; BEARER marked crit because an Entra token exceeds 255 characters), rest, salesforce, google-geocoding / loqate / melissa (NONE mode, key read by the connector), postgresql, kafka, hubspot (BEARER marked good), azure-service-bus, llm.

Page script model ftccModel - current, limit = CredentialGuide.SECRET_MAX_LENGTH (255), rows. - stored (baseUrl present; password/apiKey set and not TO_BE_SET), fieldLabels. - modes.<MODE> = {fields: {field: state}, labels: {field: contextual label}, props: [{name, state, configured}]}, from CredentialGuide.fieldStates. - text: required, optional, derived, unused, unusedPlaceholder, encrypted, counter, tooLong, endpoint, credentials, missing, none. - Script connect/connector-credentials.js?v=1.

Script behaviour - Binds once (documentElement.dataset.ftccBound). - Locates rows by id, else by position. - Follows the native authType select, or polls every 500 ms for custom drop-downs. - Mode cards set the select and dispatch change. For a non-select widget they focus and click it. - For the active mode: - Annotates each credential row with a state chip and a contextual label. - Dims unused rows and sets the placeholder Not used with this mode. - Shows the matching panes. - Updates the meter: Encrypted when saved / Too long for this field, {n} / 255 characters, based on the typed or pasted length. - Updates readiness: Endpoint ok when baseUrl is typed or stored; No credential required when every field is unused and there are no props; otherwise Credentials complete or Credentials: missing <labels>, with Too long for this field appended. - A field counts as filled when typed, or stored and not cleared by typing. - The script never submits or stores anything.

4.3.3 Mapping record form (MappingForm, declared recordForm on /root/Mapping)

  • Constants: EBX_BASE_URI = /ebx/; EBX_UI_BASE_URI = /ebx-ui/.
  • Home tab:
  • Call to action: Design the mapping graphically button (preview of MappingDesigner through /ebx/) with the hint Fields side by side, links by drag and drop, transformations, service inputs and a test on a sample record.. In creation: Save the mapping first: the graphical designer opens on an existing mapping.
  • Section What the mapping does: label, connector, operation, direction, active, description.
  • Section Where it applies: dataspace, dataset, tablePath, responseRoot. Hint: the dataspace may stay empty to apply everywhere the dataset exists, workflow child dataspaces included.
  • Tabs:
  • Inputs (service call): the ./inputs association.
  • Rules: the ./rules association.
  • Sub-objects: fields parent, collectionPath, parentLinkField, childKeyFields, childFilter, deleteMissing, plus the ./children association.
  • Import feed (if itemsPath exists): itemsPath, keyPredicate, createIfMissing.
  • Synchronization (if loadMode exists): loadMode, checkpointStrategy, checkpointInput, checkpointValuePath, checkpointInitialValue. Its hint is hard-coded in English and French, not taken from the bundle: Configure the incremental strategy. Runtime checkpoints are stored separately.
  • In creation, associations show Available once the mapping is saved.
  • Only paths present in the model are rendered. deleteOperation, maxAttempts and retryDelayMinutes are not placed on any tab.

4.3.4 Connector metrics record form (ConnectorMetricsForm, recordForm on /root/ConnectorOperationalMetrics)

  • In creation: These rows are written by the connector runtime after each execution; there is normally no need to create one by hand.
  • Summary tiles, computed with ConnectorOperationalMetrics.Snapshot (skipped if any stored value is unreadable):
  • Health badge UNKNOWN / HEALTHY / DEGRADED / FAILED. Detail: No execution recorded yet / Last success {age} / {n} consecutive failures, last one {age}. Section hint: one failure or more in a row is degraded, three or more is failed, a success resets the count (runtime rule in core).
  • Executions: requestCount, with {read} inbound · {write} outbound · {conflict} conflicts.
  • Latency p50 / p95 from the duration samples (<1000 ms shown as ms, else seconds with 1 decimal below 10 s). Detail: Over the last {n} executions, or No measurement yet.
  • Last update: age (just now, {n} min ago under 60 min, {n} h ago under 48 h, {n} days ago, never) and the date.
  • Last error (at most 600 characters) is shown when the state is DEGRADED or FAILED.
  • Sections:
  • Connector: connector, updatedAt.
  • Executions: request, read, write, skip, conflict counts.
  • Reliability: lastSuccessAt, lastFailureAt, consecutiveFailures, retry, throttle.
  • Incremental synchronization: checkpointAt, schemaDriftAt, schemaDriftCount.
  • Diagnostics: lastFailure, durationSamples.

4.3.5 Widgets and computed field

Class Bound to Behaviour
LogoWidgetFactory Connector/logo (osd:resource, via osd:defaultView/widget) Image of the resource (Logos.imagePath → module image URL; default connect/logos/default.svg), 24 px in tables and 48 px in forms. In forms the native resource widget is wrapped in a span with break-all wrapping, so long resource paths do not overflow.
UploadedLogoWidgetFactory Connector/uploadedLogo (osd:text max 360000) Table: 32 px image when the value is an accepted data URL, else —. Form: drop zone (click, Enter/Space, drag and drop), preview, status text. When editable: Browse files and Use packaged logo (clears the value) buttons. Accepts image/png, image/jpeg, image/webp, file size ≤ MAX_FILE_BYTES = 256 KB. Client errors: Choose a PNG, JPEG or WebP image., Logo exceeds the 256 KB limit., The logo could not be read.. The value is written into the hidden native widget (input + change events), so Save/Revert apply. isAcceptedDataUrl: length ≤ 360000 and a prefix data:image/png;base64,, data:image/jpeg;base64, or data:image/webp;base64, followed by content.
LogoUrlFunction Connector/logoUrl (osd:function) The uploaded data URL when accepted, else Logos.url(logo) = /ebx-connectors/www/common/images/<path>, else the default logo URL.
Logos (helper) — FOLDER = connect/logos/. Resource format ebx-connectors:ext-images:connect/logos/<file>. generatedFor(id) is non-null when id is listed in logos.txt. preferredFor("EBX") = EBX.svg (the real ON EBX mark); otherwise the generated tile.
Messages (helper) — Bundle <package>.Messages. Falls back to English, then to the key. MessageFormat applies only when arguments are given; a bad pattern returns the raw pattern. getInfo/getError return locale-lazy UserMessages.

4.4 REST API — /ebx-connectors/rest/v1 (ConnectorsResource)

Common rules - JSON responses. Authentication through the EBX REST Toolkit (Basic, token, session); the caller's session runs everything. Most handlers call ConnectorsInstaller.ensure first. - Errors use fail(status, message) → {"error": "<Redaction.redact(message)>"}. - Mapping name resolution (Mappings.mappingNamed): 1. The value is URL-decoded. 2. All digits → id. 3. Otherwise the first mapping whose label matches case-insensitively. 4. Otherwise, when the value contains / at a position > 0, connector/operation matched case-insensitively among non-child mappings, but only if exactly one matches. - Feed resolution (Mappings.feed): leading and trailing / are trimmed and the value URL-decoded. An id is accepted only if that mapping is a feed; otherwise the first feed whose label matches case-insensitively. - Record run result (enrich / sync on a record):

{runId, record, dryRun, results: [{mapping, label, connector, operation, status, message, durationMs, changes[], conflicts[]?, errors[]?}], ok, errors, conflicts, hasConflicts}

Always HTTP 200. After each mapping the record is re-read, so the next mapping sees what the previous one wrote. - Batch result (resultsResponse: feed, scope, replay):

{runId, dryRun, …extra, results: [{mapping, connector, operation, record, key, status, message, durationMs, changes[], errors[]?}], count, ok, errors, deleted}

deleted counts messages starting with deleted. HTTP 422 when errors > 0 && ok == 0 && results non-empty, else 200.

4.4.1 Endpoints

Method & path Parameters Behaviour Status codes
GET /connectors — Returns types[] (type, label, operations[{name, kind, description}]; error when a type cannot be described) and connectors[] (id, label, type, baseUrl, active, logo URL defaulting to default.svg, configured = apiKey or password not a placeholder, or authType NONE) 200
GET /templates — ConnectorTemplateService.exportTemplates() document (no credential material) 200; 400 on error
POST /templates/import body JSON importTemplates(body, actor=userId) → {changed}. Existing customer-owned templates are never overwritten. 200; 400
POST /templates/{template}/copy ?id= new id Customer-owned copy → {id, version, basedOn, basedOnVersion} 201; 400
POST /templates/{template}/upgrade ?id= Upgraded customer copy (the original copy is untouched) → same shape 201; 400
POST /connectors/openapi id, label, url, replace (false), dryRun (false); body = spec (JSON/YAML/text/any) when no url §2.3 logic → {connector, created, dryRun, baseUrl, authType, properties, operations[], notes[]} 201 when created and not dry-run, else 200; 400 ConnectorException; 500 other (message not redacted in the body, redacted in the log)
GET /mappings table (exact tablePath) All mappings (active or not, children included) → {mappings: [{id, label, connector, operation, direction, dataset, table, active, feed, child, rules}]} 200
DELETE /mappings/{mapping} id, label or connector/operation ConfigTransfer.deleteMapping → {mapping, label, deletedRows, note} 200; 404 No mapping '<x>'; 400 when the report has error
POST /lineage/declare mapping (optional id/label) DeclaredLineage.declareNow(repo, id or null): active mappings declared, inactive retired 200; 404; 400
POST /sync/{mapping} key, predicate, dryRun (false), scope, max (200), inputs Details below. see details
POST /enrich dataspace, dataset (req.), table (req.), key, predicate, mappings, connectors, dryRun (false) Details below. 400 Parameters dataset and table are required; 404 No mapping 'x'; 400 Mapping <id> (<label>) is not a mapping of <table>
POST /replay/{runId} dryRun New run; ConnectorService.replay(session, repo, runId, dryRun, "replay <runId> by <user>"); extra replayOf 200/422; 400 ConnectorException
GET /runs/{runId} — SyncLog rows of the run → {runId, calls: [{mapping, connector, operation, record, key, status, message}], count, errors, replayable = errors > 0} 200
GET /outbound mapping (required), status (default FAILED; ALL = no filter; upper-cased) OutboundState rows → {mapping, status, records: [{key, record, status, attempts, lastError, lastRunId, lastSentAt yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss}], count} 200; 404 No mapping '…' (parameter mapping is required)
GET /conflicts status (default OPEN), dataspace, dataset, table, key ConflictResolver.list → {status, count, hasConflicts, conflicts: [conflictJson]} 200
POST /conflicts/{id}/{decision} decision accept/reject (case-insensitive), comment accept writes the proposed value; reject keeps the current one → {id, ok, message, conflict} 200 when ok; 409 when the outcome is not ok; 404 No conflict <id>; 400 Decision must be accept or reject
GET /config connector, mappings (default true) ConfigTransfer.export (§4.2) 200
POST /config dryRun (false); body = export document ConfigTransfer.importConfig → report 200; 400 invalid JSON (Invalid JSON: …) or a report error
GET /webhook/{feed} — Health check, no ensure, no explicit authentication check → {mapping, label, connector, table, itemsPath, ready: true} 200; 404 No feed mapping <x>
POST /webhook/{feed} dryRun, max (10000), separator (csv); body §4.3 200/422; 400; 401; 403; 404; 500

POST /sync/{mapping} - A scope makes it an outbound scope run (runScope): - OutboundScope.parse(scope) accepts all, unsent, changed, failed, dataspaceDelta/delta; unknown → 400 Unknown scope '<x>': all, unsent, changed, failed, dataspaceDelta. - The dataset is located with DatasetLocator.find(mapping dataspace, dataset, table); 404 Dataset <ds> not found. - An optional predicate narrows the request. - sendScope(..., max, dryRun, "REST <user>"); extra {mapping, scope}. - A feed with no key and no predicate: importFrom(session, repo, mapping, runtimeInputs(inputs), max, dryRun, "REST <user>"); extra {mapping, feed: true, inputs{…}, inputsIgnored[]}. - Otherwise the record run on (mapping dataspace, dataset, table, key|predicate). - Errors: 404 No mapping '<x>'; 404 Dataset <ds> not found; 404 No record for key/predicate in <table>.

POST /enrich — mapping selection, in order: 1. mappings: comma list of names; each must apply to (dataspace, dataset, table). 2. Otherwise connectors: a comma list of id or id/operation; every INBOUND non-feed mapping of the table matching (case-insensitive) is taken, in token order. 3. Otherwise every INBOUND non-feed mapping of the table (active, non-child).

Then the record is resolved by key (primary key string) or predicate (first match), and a record run follows. An empty selection is not rejected here: it runs zero mappings (unlike MCP).

conflictJson shape: {id, status, dataspace, dataset, table, recordKey, record, field, currentValue, currentConnector, currentTrust (default 100), proposedValue, proposedConnector, proposedTrust (default 0), mapping, detectedAt, comment, resolvedBy?}.

4.4.2 Configuration export and import (ConfigTransfer)

Export

{format: "ebx-connectors-config", version: 1, connectors: [...], mappings: [...]}
  • The keys connectors and mappings are absent when the dataset is missing.
  • Connector: id plus the fields present and non-null among label, type, baseUrl, authType, username, apiKeyHeader, connectTimeoutMs, readTimeoutMs, active, trust, logo, description.
  • Also secrets: {apiKey: bool, password: bool}, where true means not a placeholder (placeholders are empty, TO_BE_SET or A_RENSEIGNER).
  • Also properties: {key: value}. A value is replaced by TO_BE_SET when the key looks secret (lower-case contains secret, password, token, privatekey, apikey, or ends with .key) and the value is neither a placeholder nor a reference (env:, file:, vault:, azurekv:).
  • Filter connector = only that id.
  • Mappings: root mappings only (osd:is-null(./parent)), filtered by connector.
  • Fields: id plus connector, operation, direction, label, description, dataspace, dataset, tablePath, responseRoot, active, collectionPath, parentLinkField, childKeyFields, childFilter, deleteMissing, itemsPath, keyPredicate, createIfMissing, loadMode, checkpointStrategy, checkpointInput, checkpointValuePath, checkpointInitialValue, deleteOperation, maxAttempts, retryDelayMinutes.
  • inputs: {name: expression}.
  • rules: [ {position, sourcePath, targetPath, transformation, policy, active, comment} ], sorted by position.
  • children: [recursive].
  • Values: booleans and ints as JSON; everything else via formatToXsString.
  • Not exported: category, uploadedLogo, username's password/apiKey (only their flags), templates, and every runtime table.

Import (importConfig) - Missing dataset → report error: the Connectors dataset is not available. - format must be ebx-connectors-config, else not an ebx-connectors-config document (missing "format": "ebx-connectors-config"). - One procedure with all privileges and triggers disabled. Dry-run throws an internal rollback exception, so nothing is committed. - Connectors are matched by id. - Create: fields applied; apiKey and password set to TO_BE_SET. - Update: only the listed fields present in the document; secrets untouched. - Without an id: note connector without id skipped. - Properties are matched by (connector, key). - Created when missing. - Updated unless the incoming value is a placeholder while the existing value is not (secrets are kept). - Root mappings are matched on parent null + connector + operation + direction + tablePath + label. - With updateById present and no match, the mapping id is used. - Created or updated. - Rules are replaced (all deleted, recreated with positions 10, 20…) when rules is an array. - Inputs are replaced when inputs is an object. - Children are matched by (parent, label), recursively. A child inherits connector, operation, direction, dataspace, dataset from the parent document when it omits them. - Afterwards ConnectorRegistry.invalidate(). - Report: {dryRun, notes[], connectorsCreated, connectorsUpdated, propertiesWritten, mappingsCreated, mappingsUpdated, rulesWritten, error?}. - Note: importConfig does not schedule declared lineage (triggers are disabled), unlike upsertMapping and deleteMapping.

upsertMapping(session, repo, document, dryRun) (MCP) - Requires connector, operation, direction, dataset, tablePath, label: mapping.<field> is required (connector, operation, direction INBOUND|OUTBOUND, dataset, tablePath, label). - The connector must exist: no connector '<id>'. - feed {itemsPath, keyPredicate, createIfMissing} is flattened onto the mapping; top-level values win. A missing keyPredicate adds the note feed ignored: a feed needs keyPredicate (…) and, when one document holds several items, itemsPath (e.g. messages[*].value). - Same import logic, triggers disabled. - After commit (non-dry-run): declared lineage is scheduled for the id, and the previous label's job is retired when updateById renamed the mapping. - Report: {dryRun, notes, mapping (id or null when dry-run), created, rulesWritten, childrenWritten, nextStep}. - childrenWritten = created + updated − 1.

deleteMapping(session, repo, id) - Triggers disabled. cascade, then delete the mapping. - Lineage: the mapping's job is retired and its former parent re-described. - Report: {mapping, label, deletedRows, note: "rules, inputs, sub-object mappings and outbound states deleted; provenance and conflict rows kept without their mapping reference"}. - Errors: mapping id is required, no mapping <id>.

cascade(ctx, dataset, id) — also used by MappingCascadeTrigger: - Recursively deletes child mappings (each with its own cascade). - Deletes MappingRule, MappingInput, SynchronizationCheckpoint, OutboundState rows with ./mapping=id. Tables absent from the model are skipped. - Sets ./mapping to null on FieldProvenance and Conflict rows. - SyncLog is untouched.

4.4.3 Inbound webhook — POST /webhook/{feed}

  1. Without an EBX session: 401 with WWW-Authenticate: Basic realm="EBX" and {"error":"Authentication required: post with the credentials of an EBX user ..."} (Webhooks.access, C2-10; the shared-secret and signature modes are removed).
  2. With a session: the same right as POST /sync (RUN, through refused("webhook", RUN)), else 403. Both come first (since 1.4.2): a caller without the right learns nothing about the mapping.
  3. Resolve the feed (404 No feed mapping <x>). Its connector must be of type webhook (400 Mapping <id> is not bound to a webhook connector) and active (403 Connector <id> is inactive). The import runs under the session of step 1.
  4. ensure, then parse the body (parseBody): - Content type containing csv, or starting with text/plain → Csv.parse(body, separator, 100000). Separator default ;; tab → \t; else the first character. - ndjson/jsonl → {rows: [...], count}. - Otherwise JSON. A missing content type is treated as JSON. - An empty body → 400 Empty or invalid body (JSON, ndjson or csv expected).
  5. importDocument(session, repo, feed, document, max, dryRun, "webhook <connectorId>").
  6. Response {mapping, dryRun, results: [{status, record, message, changes (count), conflicts[]?, errors[]?}], count, ok, errors, conflicts, hasConflicts}. HTTP 422 when errors > 0 && ok == 0, else 200. Unexpected exception → 500.

4.5 MCP

4.5.1 Transport — module endpoint /ebx-connectors/rest/mcp (McpEndpoint)

  • Enabled when -Debx.connectors.mcp is both (default) or endpoint. When disabled, POST, GET and DELETE answer 404; OPTIONS still answers 200.
  • GET and DELETE: 405 with Allow: POST,OPTIONS, produces */*, so the probe's Accept header never turns into a 406. This is what makes MCP clients fall back to plain request/response.
  • OPTIONS: 200 with Allow: POST,OPTIONS.
  • POST (consumes JSON or any):
  • No session → 401 with WWW-Authenticate: Basic realm="EBX" and {"error":"Authentication required"}.
  • Unparseable body → 400 with JSON-RPC error -32700 Parse error: … (id null).
  • Empty body → 400 -32600 Invalid request.
  • A batch array is dispatched element by element. No answers → 202; otherwise 200 with an array.
  • A single message: a notification → 202 with no body; otherwise 200 with the answer.
  • Locale = session locale, default English.
  • Protocol version constant 2025-06-18.

Methods

Method Answer
notifications/* none (202)
initialize {protocolVersion: <client's, else 2025-06-18>, capabilities: {tools: {listChanged: false}}, serverInfo: {name: "ebx-connectors", title: "ON EBX connectors", version: "1.0"}, instructions: SERVER_DESCRIPTION + " Call connectors_help first."}
ping {}
tools/list {tools: [{name, description, inputSchema: {type: object, properties: {p: {type, description}}, required: [...] (omitted when empty)}, annotations: {readOnlyHint: behavior=="read", destructiveHint: behavior=="destructive", idempotentHint: behavior!="additive"}}]}
tools/call Unknown tool → error -32602 Unknown tool: <name>. Otherwise ConnectorsTools.invoke(name, arguments) → {content: [{type: "text", text: <JSON>}], structuredContent: <JSON>, isError: <result has "error">}
resources/list / prompts/list {resources: []} / {prompts: []}
other error -32601 Method not found: <method>

4.5.2 Native runtime (ConnectorsMcpServer)

  • @McpServer("ebx-connectors"), served by EBX at /ebx-assistant/mcp/v1/ebx-connectors when EBX accepts the registration.
  • Each @McpTool method builds an argument object (null arguments are omitted) and calls the same ConnectorsTools.invoke. Result: a Map.
  • Behaviours: READ_ONLY, ADDITIVE, DESTRUCTIVE, ADDITIVE_RETRYABLE (replay, lineage). openWorldHint=true on describe, call, enrich, sync, replay, lineage and openapi.
  • Parameter type differences from the catalogue:
  • inputs (describe/call) and mapping (upsert) are Map<String,Object>.
  • config (import) is Object.
  • Booleans are Boolean; max is Integer.
  • connectors_sync has no inputs parameter in the native server (see Discrepancies).

4.5.3 Tool catalogue (ConnectorsTools.CATALOG, 23 tools)

Common rules - invoke always calls ConnectorsInstaller.ensure first. - Errors come back as {"error": "<redacted message>"}: ConnectorException and IllegalArgumentException messages as is; other exceptions are logged (redacted) and returned redacted. - String arguments accept any JSON value; container nodes are serialised to text. - Booleans accept JSON booleans or the text true. - Integers accept numbers or numeric text; an invalid value falls back to the default. - Object arguments accept a JSON object or JSON text.

Tool Behaviour tag Parameters (type, required) Behaviour and output
connectors_help read — {overview, typicalFlows[6], secrets, restEquivalent}: the discover / enrich / publish / create mapping / move configuration / OpenAPI flows. Secrets are never passed through tools (entered in the UI, or vault:, env:, file:, azurekv: references).
connectors_list read type (string) types[] (type, label, operations as "name (kind)", error), filtered by type (case-insensitive); connectors[] (id, label, type, baseUrl, authType, active, trust, secretsSet, problem?); count

Returns id, label, type, typeLabel, baseUrl, authType, trust, active, properties (values redacted), operations[] {name, kind, description, requiredInputs[], optionalInputs[]}. With an operation: fields[] {path, label, type, sample?} from describeFields(ctx, op, inputs) (it may call the service), plus fieldsOf.

No EBX data is touched, no SyncLog.

connectors_mapping_delete destructive mapping* ("Mapping id"; labels are also resolved) ConfigTransfer.deleteMapping
connectors_enrich additive dataset, table, dataspace, key, predicate, mappings, connectors, dryRun Same selection as REST /enrich, but an empty selection → No inbound mapping selected for <table>. Dataset not found → Dataset <ds> (table <t>) not found; record not found → No record for key/predicate in <table>. Origin MCP <user>. Output as the REST record run, without operation/durationMs per item; adds nextStep when conflicts exist.
connectors_sync additive mapping*, key, predicate, scope, inputs (JSON text in the description, but parsed with ConnectorService.runtimeInputs, i.e. the name=value;… format), max (integer, default 200), dryRun Feed with no key, predicate or scope → importFrom (output results + mapping, feed: true, ignoredInputs[]). No scope → delegates to enrich with mappings=<id> on the mapping's own dataspace, dataset and table. Scope → sendScope (output + mapping, scope). Batch output {runId, dryRun, results[{mapping, record, key, status, message, errors?}], count, ok, errors, nextStep when errors and not dry-run}.
connectors_replay additive runId*, dryRun runId is required; replay in a new run; output + replayOf

connectors_run_get read runId* {runId, calls[logJson], count, errors, replayable}
connectors_outbound_states read mapping*, status (SENT, FAILED default, DELETED, ALL) As REST /outbound
connectors_conflicts_list read status (default OPEN, upper-cased), dataspace, dataset, table, recordKey {status, count, hasConflicts, conflicts[]}. The conflict JSON has no resolvedBy, unlike REST.
connectors_conflict_decide additive id, decision (accept/reject), comment {id, ok, message, conflict}. Errors: No conflict <id>, decision must be accept or reject. A failed outcome is not an error result (ok=false).
connectors_log_search read mapping, connector, recordKey, status (upper-cased), runId, max (default 50, minimum effective 1) SyncLog ordered by id descending, equality filters → {rows: [{id, timestamp, mapping, connector, operation, direction, record, key, status, message, runId, origin}], count}

connectors_config_import destructive config* (catalogue: JSON text; native: object or text), dryRun ConfigTransfer.importConfig
