Items beyond the run maximum are ignored; no item gives a NO_DATA log row.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>keyPredicate</code></td> <td>Record key predicate (imports)</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=500</td> <td>XPath predicate locating the target record of each imported item, with ${json.path} placeholders resolved from the item, e.g. ./Identification/LegalEntityIdentifier='${lei}'. A non-empty value on a root mapping makes it an import feed (MappingDefinition.isFeed): it is run without a record (POST /sync/{mapping} without key, webhook, scheduler, user service) and each item is processed in its own transaction. EBX rejections are wrapped as 'Key predicate rejected by EBX on <div class="tbl"><table>: <predicate> (...)', with a hint to use date-equal() for date fields.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>createIfMissing</code></td> <td>Create the record when not found (imports)?</td> <td>boolean</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>true</code></td> <td></td> <td>Import feeds only: when the key predicate finds no record, create one (true, schema default; empty is also treated as true) or skip the item with status SKIPPED 'No record matches ... and creation is off' (false).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>loadMode</code></td> <td>Load mode</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>FULL</code></td> <td>Values: <code>FULL</code>, <code>INCREMENTAL</code></td> <td>Synchronisation policy. FULL (schema default; empty or unknown values are read as FULL): the complete source is requested. INCREMENTAL: only changes after a persisted checkpoint are meant to be requested, using checkpointStrategy. Before every call MappingSynchronizationPolicy.requireValid checks the policy against the operation descriptor and fails the call (ERROR) when unsupported: FULL must be among the operation's synchronization strategies; INCREMENTAL requires an INBOUND mapping ('Incremental load is only valid for inbound mappings'), a strategy ('Incremental load requires a checkpoint strategy') and a strategy the operation supports. Checkpoint values themselves live in the SynchronizationCheckpoint table, not here. The injection/advance logic is SynchronizationCheckpointManager; in this build no run path instantiates it (verified by search), so the setting is currently validated but not applied to the inputs.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>checkpointStrategy</code></td> <td>Checkpoint strategy</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>Values: <code>TIMESTAMP</code>, <code>MONOTONIC_ID</code>, <code>VENDOR_CURSOR</code>, <code>ODATA_DELTA</code>, <code>EBX_DELTA</code>, <code>SNAPSHOT</code></td> <td>Meaning of the incremental position when loadMode = INCREMENTAL: TIMESTAMP (source modification timestamp), MONOTONIC_ID (increasing identifier), VENDOR_CURSOR (opaque cursor/page token of the vendor API), ODATA_DELTA (OData delta link), EBX_DELTA (EBX dataspace delta), SNAPSHOT (fingerprint of a full snapshot). No schema default; ignored (forced to none) when loadMode is FULL; an unknown value is read as none. Must be supported by the operation descriptor, and must equal the strategy of an already stored checkpoint ('Stored checkpoint strategy differs from mapping policy').</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>checkpointInput</code></td> <td>Checkpoint input</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Name of the connector input that receives the last checkpoint value on an incremental run, e.g. since, after, cursor or deltaLink. Empty: the whole response. A successful incremental response without a value is an error ('Successful incremental response has no checkpoint value'); FULL runs and dry runs never advance the checkpoint.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>checkpointInitialValue</code></td> <td>Initial checkpoint value</td> <td>text</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=2000</td> <td>Optional bootstrap checkpoint used as the checkpoint input value before any SynchronizationCheckpoint row exists for the mapping (e.g. a start date). Configuration only: the runtime progress is stored separately and never written back here. Empty: the first incremental run sends no checkpoint input.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>deleteOperation</code></td> <td>Delete operation (outbound)</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=60</td> <td>Outbound mappings: name of the connector operation called for records that disappeared. With scope 'changed', a record present in OutboundState as SENT but absent from the table; with scope 'dataspaceDelta', a record present only in the dataspace's initial snapshot; with scope 'failed' or a replay, a FAILED/errored record that no longer exists. The call receives the mapping inputs resolved on the snapshot record when there is one, plus 'key' (primary key) and the scalar fields of the last document sent; success marks the OutboundState row DELETED, failure FAILED; SyncLog records it with this operation name. Empty: deletions are not propagated (ignored). Not validated against the connector's operation list at design time.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>maxAttempts</code></td> <td>Maximum attempts (outbound)</td> <td>int</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>5</code></td> <td></td> <td>Outbound retry policy: number of failed sends after which a FAILED record is no longer retried by the scopes 'changed', 'unsent' and 'failed' (counted as 'waiting') and stays FAILED until a manual replay (POST /replay/{runId}). Compared with OutboundState/attempts. Schema default 5; empty = 5; values below 1 are raised to 1.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>retryDelayMinutes</code></td> <td>Delay between attempts, minutes (outbound)</td> <td>int</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>30</code></td> <td></td> <td>Outbound retry policy: minimum number of minutes since OutboundState/lastAttemptAt before a FAILED record is retried by the scopes 'changed', 'unsent' and 'failed'; until then it is counted as waiting. Schema default 30; empty = 30; negative values are treated as 0.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>inputs</code></td> <td>Inputs</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..0</td> <td></td> <td>Association (inverse of /root/MappingInput/mapping)</td> <td>Association (inverse of MappingInput/mapping): the operation inputs of this mapping with their expressions, shown as a table on the Inputs tab of MappingForm and edited in the designer's Service call panel. Not stored on the mapping itself.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>children</code></td> <td>Sub-object mappings</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..0</td> <td></td> <td>Association (inverse of /root/Mapping/parent)</td> <td>Association (inverse of Mapping/parent): the sub-object mappings of this mapping (child tables fed from collections of the document), shown on the Sub-objects tab. Only active children are applied at run time. Not stored on the mapping itself.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>rules</code></td> <td>Rules</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..0</td> <td></td> <td>Association (inverse of /root/MappingRule/mapping)</td> <td>Association (inverse of MappingRule/mapping): the field rules of this mapping, applied in position order, shown on the Rules tab and drawn in the graphical designer. Not stored on the mapping itself.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-mappinginput">Table <code>MappingInput</code> — Mapping inputs</h3> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> One input value passed to the connector operation when a mapping runs: the input name the operation expects and an expression computing its value, typically from fields of the EBX record being synchronised, run-time parameters or constants (e.g. lei = ${./Identification/LegalEntityIdentifier}).</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Created and edited by users in the mapping form's Inputs tab or the designer (MappingDesignerUserService 'Save the rules' rewrites the inputs of the mapping; 'Create the reverse mapping' copies them), by seeding (ConnectorsInstaller), and by configuration import / connectors_mapping_upsert (ConfigTransfer replaces all inputs of the mapping when 'inputs' is given). Deleted when its mapping is deleted (MappingCascadeTrigger via ConfigTransfer.cascade). MappingPartLineageTrigger re-describes the mapping's declared lineage after every create, modify or delete. Read-only for the engine.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Composite primary key (/mapping, /name): one expression per input name per mapping. Default label is the input name.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/MappingInput</code>; primary key <code>/mapping /name</code>; record label <code>${./name}</code>; trigger <code>MappingPartLineageTrigger</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>mapping</code></td> <td>Mapping</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>Foreign key → /root/Mapping</td> <td>Foreign key to the Mapping this input belongs to; part of the primary key. Rows whose mapping id cannot be resolved are ignored when mappings are loaded. Used by MappingInputConstraint to find the table against which ${./...} placeholders are checked.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>name</code></td> <td>Input name</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=60</td> <td>Name of the operation input, as declared by the connector type (e.g. lei, siren, query, tables, since); part of the primary key, max 60. Blank names are ignored. Operation inputs declared as required must resolve to a non-empty value, otherwise the record call is SKIPPED with 'Input '<name>' is empty on this record'. Not validated against the operation's input list in the schema.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>expression</code></td> <td>Expression</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=500; Constraint: MappingInputConstraint</td> <td>Expression resolved at run time (Expressions.resolve) to produce the input value, then trimmed. Syntax: ${./Group/field} = value of a record field in XML Schema format (ISO dates, foreign key = key); text around placeholders is concatenated; ${param.name} = a run-time parameter (workflow, API, scheduler 'inputs'; for feeds only names read this way are accepted, others are reported as ignored); a literal without placeholders is a constant; ${./field|trans1|trans2} applies a transformation pipeline to one placeholder, and 'expr |> pipeline' to the assembled value; placeholders can be nested inside transformation arguments. Available transformations are those of rules (trim, upper, lower, capitalize, left, right, substring, replace, regex, vatfr, digits, prefix, suffix, default, constant, nullifempty, nullif, isodate, isodatetime, date, map, join, first, count, boolean, number, round, fk, lookup); an unknown one fails the call. For feeds (no record), record placeholders resolve empty. MappingInputConstraint warns (never blocks) 'Field(s) [...] are not fields of <div class="tbl"><table>: the input will be empty.'</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-mappingrule">Table <code>MappingRule</code> — Mapping rules</h3> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> One field rule of a mapping: where a value is read (sourcePath), the transformation pipeline applied to it, where it is written (targetPath) and, inbound, under which write policy. Inbound the source is a JSON path in the connector document and the target an EBX field; outbound the source is an EBX field and the target a JSON path of the document being built. A rule without source produces a constant from its pipeline.</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Created and edited by users in the Rules tab or, mostly, in the graphical designer (MappingDesignerUserService 'Save the rules' replaces the rules with positions 10, 20, ... and active = true; 'Create the reverse mapping' copies them with source/target swapped and policy IF_EMPTY), by seeding (ConnectorsInstaller), and by configuration import / connectors_mapping_upsert (ConfigTransfer deletes and recreates all rules of the mapping, positions 10, 20, ...). Deleted with their mapping by MappingCascadeTrigger. MappingPartLineageTrigger re-describes the mapping's declared lineage (column lineage) after every change. Read by ConnectorRepository.mappings() sorted by position; inactive rules are skipped.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Primary key /id, auto-incremented integer. Default label '${position} ${sourcePath} -> ${targetPath}'. Rules are not addressed by id in the APIs; export/import carries them as an ordered list under their mapping.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/MappingRule</code>; primary key <code>/id</code>; record label <code>${./position} ${./sourcePath} -> ${./targetPath}</code>; trigger <code>MappingPartLineageTrigger</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>id</code></td> <td>Id</td> <td>id (int)</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>autoIncrement</td> <td>Technical auto-incremented identifier of the rule, assigned by EBX. Not used for matching on import (rules are replaced as a whole).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>mapping</code></td> <td>Mapping</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>Foreign key → /root/Mapping</td> <td>Foreign key to the Mapping owning the rule. Rules whose mapping id cannot be resolved are ignored at load. Also used by MappingRulePathConstraint to know the mapping's direction and table.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>position</code></td> <td>Position</td> <td>int</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>10</code></td> <td></td> <td>Evaluation order of the rule within its mapping (ascending; schema default 10; empty is read as 0). Order matters inbound because when several rules target the same EBX field, the first rule producing a non-empty value wins (fallback chains). Designer and import renumber rules 10, 20, 30...</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>sourcePath</code></td> <td>Source path</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=500; Constraint: MappingRulePathConstraint</td> <td>Where the value is read. Inbound: dotted JSON path in the document after responseRoot (for a sub-object mapping, relative to the collection element): entity.legalName.name, results[0].name (index), addressLines[*] (all elements as a list, to be reduced with join/first/count). Outbound: EBX path relative to the record, e.g. ./Group/field. Empty: no source, the rule's value comes only from its pipeline (e.g. constant(GLEIF)). MappingRulePathConstraint checks it only when it is the EBX side (outbound) and warns '<path> is not a field of <div class="tbl"><table>: this rule will fail.'</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>targetPath</code></td> <td>Target path</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=500; Constraint: MappingRulePathConstraint</td> <td>Where the value is written. Inbound: EBX field path relative to the record (./LegalInformation/legalName); a foreign key receives the key of the referenced record (composite key as a|b); values are set from XML Schema strings (dates 2026-09-05, booleans true/false, decimals with a dot). A target that is not a terminal field of the table gives the error '<path>: not a field of <div class="tbl"><table>'. Outbound: JSON path of the document being built (primaryAddress.addressLocality.name). Mandatory, max 500. MappingRulePathConstraint warns when the EBX side is not a field of the mapping's table.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>transformation</code></td> <td>Transformation</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=500</td> <td>Optional pipeline applied to the source value, steps separated by '|', arguments in parentheses separated by commas (quote arguments containing a comma, a '|' or edge spaces). Registered steps (Transformations): trim, upper, lower, capitalize, left(n), right(n), substring(start,end), replace(a,b), regex(pattern,group) (null when no match), digits, vatfr (SIREN/SIRET to French VAT number), prefix(p), suffix(s), default(v), constant(v), nullifempty, nullif(v), isodate, isodatetime, date(inFormat,outFormat), map(A=B;C=D;*=default), join(sep), first, count, boolean, number, round(n), fk(/root/Table[,dataspace,dataset]) (keeps the value only if the referenced record exists), lookup(/root/Table,./field,./result[,dataspace,dataset]). Additional steps can be registered in Java. An unknown step or a failing step records an error for that target; an empty result means the rule writes nothing.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>policy</code></td> <td>Write policy</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>IF_EMPTY</code></td> <td>maxLength=40; Values: <code>IF_EMPTY</code> (Only when the field is empty), <code>ALWAYS</code> (Always (overwrite)), <code>TRUSTED</code> (Trusted (write when empty, or when this source is at least as trusted as the one that wrote the value; otherwise log a conflict))</td> <td>Inbound write policy for an existing record. IF_EMPTY (schema default; empty/unknown values are read as IF_EMPTY): write only when the EBX field is empty, otherwise skip. ALWAYS: overwrite whatever is there (an identical value is skipped). TRUSTED: write when the field is empty, or when the connector's trust (Connector/trust) is at least the trust of whoever wrote the current value according to FieldProvenance (a value with no provenance, i.e. entered by hand or loaded outside the connectors, counts as 100); otherwise nothing is written and a Conflict row (status OPEN) is logged for a steward. On record creation (feeds, child rows) all policies write. Whatever the policy, every value actually written is recorded in FieldProvenance. Not meaningful for outbound rules.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>active</code></td> <td>Active?</td> <td>boolean</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>true</code></td> <td></td> <td>Whether the rule is applied (schema default true; empty is treated as active). Inactive rules are excluded when the mapping is loaded, so they neither write nor appear in the designer's rule list.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>comment</code></td> <td>Comment</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=500</td> <td>Free note on the rule (max 500), editable in the designer's inspector, returned by connectors_mapping_get and exported/imported with the configuration. Not used by the synchronisation engine.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-fieldprovenance">Table <code>FieldProvenance</code> — Field provenance</h3> <p><em>Schema description:</em> Who wrote each field of a record and with which trust; read by the TRUSTED policy, kept as an audit trail.</p> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> Records, for each field of each EBX record written by a connector, which connector (and mapping) last wrote it, with the trust level that source had and the value written. It is the audit trail of connector writes and the source of truth read by the TRUSTED rule policy to decide whether a new proposal may overwrite the current value.</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Written only by the module engine, never by users in normal operation: ConnectorRepository.recordProvenance upserts one row per changed main-record field (paths starting with ./; child-row changes are not recorded) after every non-dry-run inbound record synchronisation (ConnectorService.syncRecord) and feed item import, whatever the rule policy, and ConflictResolver.accept records the accepted proposal as the new provenance. Writes run with all privileges and failures are only logged. Rows are not deleted by retention; when their mapping is deleted, MappingCascadeTrigger only clears /mapping. Read by ConnectorRepository.provenanceOf (TrustOracle) during TRUSTED evaluation.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Primary key /id, auto-incremented. Logically unique on (dataspace, dataset, tablePath, recordKey, fieldPath): the engine looks up that combination and updates the existing row instead of creating a second one. Default label '${fieldPath} = ${value} (${connector}, ${trust})'.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/FieldProvenance</code>; primary key <code>/id</code>; record label <code>${./fieldPath} = ${./value} (${./connector}, ${./trust})</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>id</code></td> <td>Id</td> <td>id (int)</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>autoIncrement</td> <td>Technical auto-incremented identifier. Carries no business meaning; the logical key is dataspace + dataset + tablePath + recordKey + fieldPath.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>dataspace</code></td> <td>Dataspace</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Name of the dataspace of the written record (the actual dataspace, e.g. a workflow child dataspace, not the mapping's restriction). Part of the logical key used by the TRUSTED lookup, so provenance recorded in a child dataspace is not seen from another dataspace. Written by the engine.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>dataset</code></td> <td>Dataset</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Name of the dataset of the written record. Part of the logical key. Written by the engine.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>tablePath</code></td> <td>Table</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Absolute path of the table of the written record (e.g. /root/Companies). Part of the logical key. Written by the engine.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>recordKey</code></td> <td>Record key</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Primary key of the written record in EBX formatted form (PrimaryKey.format(), composite keys as a|b). Part of the logical key. Written by the engine.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>fieldPath</code></td> <td>Field</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Path of the written field relative to the record, as used by the rule target (e.g. ./LegalInformation/legalName). Part of the logical key; only paths starting with ./ are recorded. Written by the engine.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>connector</code></td> <td>Written by</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>Foreign key → /root/Connector</td> <td>Connector (foreign key to Connector) that last wrote the value, i.e. the current provenance of the field. Reported as 'current value written by' when a TRUSTED conflict is logged. Written by the engine (or, on conflict acceptance, the proposing connector).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>mapping</code></td> <td>Mapping</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>Foreign key → /root/Mapping</td> <td>Mapping (foreign key) through which the value was written, as the mapping id. Emptied by MappingCascadeTrigger when the mapping is deleted, the provenance row itself being kept. Informational for the TRUSTED decision (only connector and trust are read).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>trust</code></td> <td>Trust of the source</td> <td>int</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Trust level (0-100) of the writing connector at the time of the write (Connector/trust, 50 when the connector has none; for an accepted conflict, the trust the proposal had). The TRUSTED policy compares a new source's current trust with this stored value (empty is read as 50); a field without any provenance row counts as trust 100 (manual entry).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>value</code></td> <td>Value written</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=2000</td> <td>Value written, as an XML Schema string, truncated to 2000 characters. Audit information; not re-read by the TRUSTED policy (which compares against the live record value).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>writtenAt</code></td> <td>Written at</td> <td>dateTime</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Date and time of the last write of this field by a connector (server time). Updated on every new write. Written by the engine.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-conflict">Table <code>Conflict</code> — Conflicts</h3> <p><em>Schema description:</em> A less trusted source proposed a different value for a field written by a more trusted one (TRUSTED policy): the proposal is kept here for a steward to decide.</p> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> A proposal refused by the TRUSTED write policy: a connector less trusted than the author of the current value proposed a different value for a field of an EBX record. The row keeps both values, their authors and trust levels so that a data steward can decide to apply the proposal or keep the current value.</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Created by the engine (ConnectorRepository.logConflicts) after a non-dry-run inbound record synchronisation or feed item import whose TRUSTED rules produced conflicts; a conflict with the same dataset, table, record key, field and proposed value that is still OPEN is not logged twice. Decided by a steward through the 'Manage connector conflicts' user service (ConflictManagerUserService, on a dataspace, table, record, the Conflict table itself, or as workflow user task ebx-connectors@ConflictManager), REST POST /conflicts/{id}/accept|reject, or MCP connectors_conflict_decide — all via ConflictResolver, which sets status, comment, resolvedBy and resolvedAt under a per-conflict lock and refuses a conflict that is no longer OPEN. Accepting writes the proposed value into the record and records its provenance. Deleted by retention (ebx.connectors.log.retentionDays > 0: decided conflicts, status not OPEN, older than N days by detectedAt, at most hourly and 2000 rows). When the mapping is deleted, MappingCascadeTrigger clears /mapping only.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Primary key /id, auto-incremented; the id used by POST /conflicts/{id}/accept|reject and connectors_conflict_decide. Default label '${fieldPath}: ${currentValue} vs ${proposedValue} (${proposedConnector})'.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/Conflict</code>; primary key <code>/id</code>; record label <code>${./fieldPath}: ${./currentValue} vs ${./proposedValue} (${./proposedConnector})</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>id</code></td> <td>Id</td> <td>id (int)</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>autoIncrement</td> <td>Technical auto-incremented identifier of the conflict, used by the REST decision endpoints and the MCP decision tool.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>status</code></td> <td>Status</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>OPEN</code></td> <td>maxLength=40; Values: <code>OPEN</code> (Open), <code>ACCEPTED</code> (Accepted (proposed value applied by hand)), <code>REJECTED</code> (Rejected (current value kept))</td> <td>Decision state. OPEN (schema default, set on creation): awaiting a steward; the only status listed by default and the only one that can be decided. ACCEPTED: the proposed value was written into the record by ConflictResolver.accept and the proposing connector became the field's provenance. REJECTED: the current value was kept. Listing APIs accept status=ACCEPTED|REJECTED for history. Only non-OPEN conflicts are purged by retention.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>dataspace</code></td> <td>Dataspace</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Dataspace of the record concerned (actual dataspace of the synchronised record). Used by ConflictResolver to locate the record when accepting, and as a filter of the conflict lists (service on a dataspace, REST/MCP filter). Written by the engine.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>dataset</code></td> <td>Dataset</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Dataset of the record concerned. Used to locate the record, to filter lists and in the duplicate check of open conflicts. Written by the engine.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>tablePath</code></td> <td>Table</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Absolute path of the table of the record concerned. Used to locate the record, filter lists (service on a table, table= parameter) and in the duplicate check. Written by the engine.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>recordKey</code></td> <td>Record key</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Formatted primary key of the record concerned (composite keys as a|b). Used to locate the record (an accept on a record that no longer exists fails 'record ... no longer exists'), to filter lists (service on a record, key= parameter) and in the duplicate check.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>recordLabel</code></td> <td>Record</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Label of the record at detection time (default locale), truncated to 255 characters, for display in the conflict manager and API results. Informational.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>fieldPath</code></td> <td>Field</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Path of the field in conflict relative to the record (e.g. ./Identification/Name). The accepted value is written to this path. Part of the duplicate check.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>currentValue</code></td> <td>Current value</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=2000</td> <td>Value of the field in the record when the conflict was detected, as an XML Schema string. Shown to the steward and returned in the rejection message; not re-read on acceptance (the live value is overwritten).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>currentConnector</code></td> <td>Current value written by</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Connector id that wrote the current value according to FieldProvenance. Empty: no provenance, i.e. the value was entered by hand or loaded outside the connectors (trust 100). Plain text, not a foreign key.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>currentTrust</code></td> <td>Trust of the current value</td> <td>int</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Trust of the current value used in the decision: the trust stored in FieldProvenance, or 100 when there is no provenance. Informational for the steward.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>proposedValue</code></td> <td>Proposed value</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=2000</td> <td>Value the less trusted connector proposed, as an XML Schema string. Written into the record (setValueFromXsString; empty = null) when the conflict is accepted. Part of the duplicate check (a different proposal for the same field opens a new conflict).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>proposedConnector</code></td> <td>Proposed by</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>Foreign key → /root/Connector</td> <td>Connector (foreign key) that made the refused proposal. On acceptance it becomes the field's provenance and is quoted in the session tracking info 'Connector conflict accepted: <connector>'.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>proposedTrust</code></td> <td>Trust of the proposal</td> <td>int</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Trust level of the proposing connector at detection time. On acceptance it is the trust recorded in FieldProvenance (falling back to the connector's current trust if empty).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>mapping</code></td> <td>Mapping</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>Foreign key → /root/Mapping</td> <td>Mapping (foreign key, mapping id) whose TRUSTED rule produced the proposal. Reused as the mapping of the provenance row on acceptance. Emptied by MappingCascadeTrigger when the mapping is deleted, the conflict being kept.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>detectedAt</code></td> <td>Detected at</td> <td>dateTime</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Date and time the conflict was logged. Lists are sorted most recent first on it, and retention purges decided conflicts older than ebx.connectors.log.retentionDays by this date.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>comment</code></td> <td>Steward comment</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=500</td> <td>Steward comment stored with the decision (max 500, truncated by code), entered as the common comment of the conflict manager screen or the comment= parameter of the REST/MCP decision. Only written when non-blank.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>resolvedBy</code></td> <td>Resolved by</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>EBX user id of the session that accepted or rejected the conflict. Written by ConflictResolver at decision time; empty while OPEN.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>resolvedAt</code></td> <td>Resolved at</td> <td>dateTime</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Date and time of the decision. Written by ConflictResolver; empty while OPEN.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-connectoroperationalmetrics">Table <code>ConnectorOperationalMetrics</code> — Connector health and metrics</h3> <p><em>Schema description:</em> Durable health, traffic, latency, checkpoint and schema-drift telemetry. Java 64-bit counters are stored as EBX-compatible decimal strings.</p> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> Durable health and telemetry of one connector: execution counters (inbound, outbound, skipped, conflicts, total), a rolling window of execution durations for latency percentiles, the last success and failure, the consecutive-failure streak that determines health, and checkpoint and schema-drift timestamps. It is displayed by the ConnectorMetricsForm record form. 64-bit counters are stored as decimal strings.</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Created on the first observed execution of a connector and updated after each one by the engine. ConnectorsModuleRegistration installs ConnectorOperationsRuntime.MetricsAndAlertsObserver at startup, which applies ConnectorOperationalMetrics rules and persists them through EbxConnectorOperationalMetricsPersistence under a system session named 'connectors-metrics'. Each SyncResult reported through ConnectorOperationsRuntime.observe triggers success() or failure(); checkpoint and schema-drift signals update their own fields. Observation failures are logged and never fail the connector work. Not meant to be edited by users; never deleted by code.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Primary key is the connector id (foreign key to Connector), one row per connector. The record label is the connector id.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/ConnectorOperationalMetrics</code>; primary key <code>/connector</code>; record label <code>${./connector}</code>; record form <code>ConnectorMetricsForm</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>connector</code></td> <td>Connector</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>Foreign key → /root/Connector</td> <td>Connector these figures describe (foreign key to Connector.id). Mandatory; unique.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>readCount</code></td> <td>Inbound executions</td> <td>metricCounter (string)</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>0</code></td> <td>pattern=[0-9]+; maxLength=19</td> <td>Cumulative number of executions whose direction was INBOUND or IMPORT (reads from the external application, including import feeds). Non-negative decimal string (pattern [0-9]+, at most 19 digits), saturating at Long.MAX_VALUE. Schema default 0; an empty value is read as 0. Engine-written.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-connectorgovernance">Table <code>ConnectorGovernance</code> — Connector governance</h3> <p><em>Schema description:</em> Ownership, source precedence, approval, effective dates, publication version, dependencies and secret references.</p> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> Governance metadata attached to a governed connector or mapping configuration (identified by a resource id): owner, source system and precedence, approval state, effective period, publication version, dependencies on other resources, and references (never values) to the secrets it uses. Never deleted by code.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Primary key is resourceId, the identifier of the governed connector or mapping configuration (free string, max 255). Only the number of references and the provider names are ever exposed (describe, audit detail). Max 16000.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-connectorgovernanceaudit">Table <code>ConnectorGovernanceAudit</code> — Connector governance audit</h3> <p><em>Schema description:</em> Append-only, secret-safe governance transitions.</p> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> Append-only audit trail of governance transitions on governed connector or mapping configurations: who did what, when, with a secret-safe detail. It is the history behind ConnectorGovernance.</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Rows are appended by ConnectorGovernanceService (via EbxConnectorGovernanceStore.appendAudit) on every register, submit, approve, reject and publish; they are never updated or deleted by code. They are read, filtered by resource and sorted by occurredAt, by ConnectorGovernanceApplicationService.audit, which requires the AUDIT permission. As for ConnectorGovernance, the governance services are not wired to any runtime entry point in the current source, so the table is not populated by the deployed module.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Primary key is an auto-incremented integer id. The record label is '<resourceId> <action>'.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/ConnectorGovernanceAudit</code>; primary key <code>/id</code>; record label <code>${./resourceId} ${./action}</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>id</code></td> <td></td> <td>id (int)</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>autoIncrement</td> <td>Technical identifier, auto-incremented by EBX when the audit row is created.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>resourceId</code></td> <td></td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Identifier of the governed resource the event concerns (the ConnectorGovernance.resourceId value; not a declared foreign key). Used to sort the audit history. Mandatory.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-executionrun">Table <code>ExecutionRun</code> — Execution runs</h3> <p><em>Schema description:</em> Durable restart, item-attempt, dead-letter and lease state for bounded connector executions.</p> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> Durable state of a bounded, restartable connector execution identified by a run id: the index of the next work item to process, per-item attempt counters, the items already completed, the items sent to dead letter, and an exclusive lease so that only one worker processes the run at a time. It is the EBX persistence of the RecoverableExecution / DurableExecutionStateStore framework.</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Designed to be written only by code: EbxRecoverableExecutionStore (DurableExecutionStateStore.Persistence) creates the row when a lease is first acquired (nextIndex 0), updates it on every checkpoint save and clears leaseOwner/leaseExpiresAt when the lease is released; OperationalExecutionService acquires a 5-minute lease around RecoverableExecution.run. In this build no production code path instantiates EbxRecoverableExecutionStore or OperationalExecutionService (only unit tests use the in-memory persistence), so the table is normally empty. Nothing in the module deletes rows (no retention, no cascade).</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Primary key /runId (string, max 160), also the default label. The framework requires a non-blank run id; work item ids must match [A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,160}.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/ExecutionRun</code>; primary key <code>/runId</code>; record label <code>${./runId}</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>runId</code></td> <td></td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=160</td> <td>Identifier of the restartable execution; primary key. A checkpoint loaded for a run must belong to that run ('Checkpoint belongs to another run'). Written by EbxRecoverableExecutionStore.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>nextIndex</code></td> <td></td> <td>int</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Zero-based position in the ordered work source from which a resumed run continues: items before it are skipped on restart. Set to 0 when the row is created by a lease, then advanced as items reach a terminal state; empty is read as 0.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>attempts</code></td> <td></td> <td>text</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=16000</td> <td>Per-item attempt counters serialised as comma-separated 'itemId=count' pairs (e.g. rec-1=2,rec-7=1), used to stop retrying an item after the policy's maximum attempts (hard cap 10). Written by the store; max 16000 characters.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>completed</code></td> <td></td> <td>text</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=16000</td> <td>Comma-separated ids of the work items that reached a successful terminal state; a resumed run skips them (idempotent replay).
