Another owner cannot acquire the lease while leaseExpiresAt is in the future ('Execution is already leased'); the same owner may renew it. Cleared when the owner releases the lease.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>leaseExpiresAt</code></td> <td></td> <td>dateTime</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Expiry date-time of the current lease (acquisition time plus the requested duration, at most 1 hour; OperationalExecutionService uses 5 minutes). After it, another worker may take over the run. Cleared on release.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>updatedAt</code></td> <td></td> <td>dateTime</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Date-time of the last write of the row (lease acquisition or state save). Written by the store; informational.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-outboundstate">Table <code>OutboundState</code> — Outbound deliveries</h3> <p><em>Schema description:</em> What was sent to the external application by each outbound mapping: fingerprint of the last document (scope "changed" resends only what differs), failures to replay, deletions.</p> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> Delivery state of one EBX record for one outbound mapping: whether its last delivery was sent, failed or was a deletion, the SHA-256 fingerprint and content of the last document sent successfully, the number of failed attempts since the last success, and the run involved. It drives the outbound scopes 'unsent', 'changed' and 'failed', the retry policy and the propagation of deletions.</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Written only by the engine (ConnectorRepository.markOutbound, all privileges, failures only logged) after each non-dry-run outbound call on a root (non sub-object) mapping: status FAILED on an ERROR (attempts + 1, lastError), SENT on success with a built document (attempts reset to 0, hash and lastDocument updated, lastSentAt set); SKIPPED calls and dry runs leave it untouched. ConnectorService.deleteRemote writes DELETED (success) or FAILED for deleted records. Read by OutboundSelector, replay, REST GET /outbound and MCP connectors_outbound_states. Deleted with its mapping by MappingCascadeTrigger; no retention.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Composite primary key (/mapping, /recordKey): one row per outbound mapping and record. Default label '${recordLabel} (${status})'.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/OutboundState</code>; primary key <code>/mapping /recordKey</code>; record label <code>${./recordLabel} (${./status})</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>mapping</code></td> <td>Mapping</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>Foreign key → /root/Mapping</td> <td>Foreign key to the outbound Mapping (mapping id); part of the primary key. Used to list the states of a mapping (?mapping= on GET /outbound).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>recordKey</code></td> <td>Record key</td> <td>string</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Formatted primary key of the EBX record (composite keys as a|b), truncated to 255; part of the primary key. Used to look the record up again (scope 'failed', deletion detection, replay) — a key absent from the table marks the record as deleted.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>recordLabel</code></td> <td>Record</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Label of the record at the last delivery (truncated to 255), kept so that deletions and failures of records that no longer exist can still be displayed and logged. Only overwritten when a label is available.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>dataspace</code></td> <td>Dataspace</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Dataspace restriction copied from the mapping at each write (empty when the mapping applies to any dataspace). Informational; not used to select states.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>dataset</code></td> <td>Dataset</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Dataset copied from the mapping at each write. Informational.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>tablePath</code></td> <td>Table</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Table path copied from the mapping at each write. Informational.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>status</code></td> <td>Status</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=40; Values: <code>SENT</code> (Sent), <code>FAILED</code> (Failed (to replay)), <code>DELETED</code> (Deletion sent)</td> <td>Last delivery outcome. SENT: the document was sent successfully (fingerprint and document stored). FAILED: the last send or deletion failed; retried by scopes 'changed', 'unsent' and 'failed' while attempts < Mapping/maxAttempts and Mapping/retryDelayMinutes have elapsed, otherwise waiting until a manual replay. DELETED: the record disappeared and the deleteOperation call succeeded. No schema default (always set by the engine). Scope 'changed' also treats SENT rows whose record is gone as deletions when the mapping has a deleteOperation.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>lastSentAt</code></td> <td>Last successful send</td> <td>dateTime</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Date-time of the last successful delivery (SENT or DELETED). Empty means the record was never sent successfully, which is what scope 'unsent' selects and what makes scope 'changed' resend it regardless of fingerprint.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>lastAttemptAt</code></td> <td>Last attempt</td> <td>dateTime</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Date-time of the last delivery attempt, successful or not. The retry delay (Mapping/retryDelayMinutes) is measured from it.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>attempts</code></td> <td>Failed attempts since the last success</td> <td>int</td> <td>0..1</td> <td><code>0</code></td> <td></td> <td>Number of failed attempts since the last success: incremented on each FAILED write, reset to 0 on SENT or DELETED. Schema default 0. When it reaches Mapping/maxAttempts the record is no longer retried automatically.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>hash</code></td> <td>Fingerprint of the sent document</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=64</td> <td>SHA-256 hex fingerprint (64 characters) of the last document sent successfully; updated only on successful sends. Scope 'changed' rebuilds the outbound document for each record and resends it only if its fingerprint differs from this value (so a change made by an inbound enrichment is resent too).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>lastDocument</code></td> <td>Last document sent</td> <td>text</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=8000</td> <td>JSON text of the last document sent successfully (truncated to 8000 characters). On a deletion its scalar fields are passed to the deleteOperation call as inputs, alongside the key.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>lastError</code></td> <td>Last error</td> <td>text</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=2000</td> <td>Error message of the last failed attempt (truncated to 2000); cleared on success.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>lastRunId</code></td> <td>Last run</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=40</td> <td>Run id (format RyyyyMMdd-HHmmss-nnnn) of the execution that last wrote this state, linking it to the SyncLog rows of that run and to POST /replay/{runId}.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h3 id="table-synclog">Table <code>SyncLog</code> — Synchronisation log</h3> <p><strong>Purpose.</strong> The synchronisation log: one row per connector call on a record, feed item, outbound delivery, remote deletion or feed-level outcome, with its status, message, changes, duration, user, origin and run id. It is the operational history of the module and the basis for replaying the failures of a run.</p> <p><strong>Life cycle.</strong> Appended only by the engine through ConnectorRepository.log (all privileges; errors swallowed so logging never breaks a sync) for every non-dry-run call from the user service (ConnectorSyncUserService), REST API, workflow bean, scheduled task, webhook, MCP tools and replays. Dry runs (simulation, designer test) and synchronisations run inside a table trigger (ConnectorService.syncRecordInProcedure) write no row. Rows are never updated. Retention: with the JVM property ebx.connectors.log.retentionDays = N > 0, rows older than N days (by timestamp) are deleted, at most once an hour and 2000 rows per pass, triggered by the next log write. Not affected by mapping deletion. Read by replay (ConnectorService.replay), GET /runs/{runId}, connectors_run_get, connectors_log_search and the connector operations dashboard.</p> <p><strong>Identification.</strong> Primary key /id, auto-incremented (the MCP log search sorts by it, most recent first). Default label '${timestamp} ${connector}/${operation} ${recordLabel} ${status}'. Rows of one execution are grouped by /runId.</p> <p><strong>Technical properties.</strong> path <code>/root/SyncLog</code>; primary key <code>/id</code>; record label <code>${./timestamp} ${./connector}/${./operation} ${./recordLabel} ${./status}</code>.</p> <div class="tbl"><table> <thead> <tr> <th>Attribute</th> <th>Label</th> <th>Type</th> <th>Card.</th> <th>Default</th> <th>Constraints</th> <th>Definition</th> </tr> </thead> <tbody> <tr> <td><code>id</code></td> <td>Id</td> <td>id (int)</td> <td>1..1</td> <td></td> <td>autoIncrement</td> <td>Technical auto-incremented identifier of the log row; increasing order approximates chronological order.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>timestamp</code></td> <td>Timestamp</td> <td>dateTime</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Date-time the row was written (end of the call). Used by retention (rows older than ebx.connectors.log.retentionDays are purged).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>connector</code></td> <td>Connector</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=40</td> <td>Id of the connector called (plain text, not a foreign key, so the log survives connector deletion). Empty on replay placeholder rows for a mapping that no longer exists.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>operation</code></td> <td>Operation</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=60</td> <td>Name of the operation called; for a remote deletion, the mapping's deleteOperation.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>direction</code></td> <td>Direction</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=10</td> <td>Kind of call: INBOUND (record enrichment), OUTBOUND (send or remote deletion), IMPORT (feed items and feed-level header rows). Empty on replay placeholder rows. Plain text, not an enumeration.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>dataspace</code></td> <td>Dataspace</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>Dataspace where the call ran: the record's actual dataspace for record calls, the resolved dataset's dataspace for feeds, the mapping's restriction for outbound scope notes and deletions. Replay uses it to find the dataset of a failed record again.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>tablePath</code></td> <td>Table</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Absolute path of the table concerned.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>recordKey</code></td> <td>Record key</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Formatted primary key of the record (composite a|b), truncated to 255; for a feed item, the resolved key predicate. Replay re-runs the mapping on the record with this key (skipping it if the record no longer exists, or deleting it remotely when the outbound mapping has a deleteOperation).</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>recordLabel</code></td> <td>Record</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=255</td> <td>Label of the record (or, for feeds, the resolved predicate or the feed label on header rows), truncated to 255. Informational.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>status</code></td> <td>Status</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=20</td> <td>Outcome of the call (SyncResult.Status): OK = at least one change written (or document sent); NO_CHANGE = call succeeded, nothing to write; NO_DATA = the service returned nothing for these inputs (or a feed had no item); SKIPPED = a required input was empty, no record matched with creation off, no applicable mapping, or the record/mapping of a replayed call disappeared; ERROR = the service or the mapping failed. DRY_RUN exists in code but dry runs are not logged. Only ERROR rows are replayed by POST /replay/{runId}.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>message</code></td> <td>Message</td> <td>text</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=2000</td> <td>Human-readable result or cause of failure (e.g. 'Input 'lei' is empty on this record', '2 change(s), 0 skipped', 'deleted remotely (delete)'), passed through Redaction to mask secrets and truncated to 2000 characters.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>changes</code></td> <td>Changes</td> <td>text</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=4000</td> <td>Text list of the changes made or of the values sent (path: old -> new), redacted and truncated to 4000 characters. Left empty when the JVM property ebx.connectors.log.storeChanges=false, to keep values and documents (personal data) out of the log.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>durationMs</code></td> <td>Duration (ms)</td> <td>int</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td></td> <td>Duration of the call in milliseconds, summed by the operations dashboard.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>user</code></td> <td>User</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=120</td> <td>EBX user id of the session that ran the call; empty when the session has no user.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>origin</code></td> <td>Origin</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=60</td> <td>What started the call, as passed by the caller: 'userService' (Synchronise user service), 'REST <user>' (REST API), 'workflow <user>' (ConnectorScriptTaskBean), 'scheduler' (ConnectorScheduledTask), 'MCP <user>' (MCP tools), 'webhook <connectorId>' (POST /webhook), 'replay <runId> by <user>' (REST replay). Free text, not an enumeration.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>runId</code></td> <td>Run</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=40</td> <td>Identifier of the execution the call belongs to, format RyyyyMMdd-HHmmss-nnnn (ConnectorService.beginRun: one REST call, workflow step, scheduled run or user service run; a single call outside a run gets its own id). POST /ebx-connectors/rest/v1/replay/{runId} re-runs the ERROR rows of the run, GET /runs/{runId} lists them. Only written when the model has the field.</td> </tr> <tr> <td><code>mapping</code></td> <td>Mapping</td> <td>string</td> <td>0..1</td> <td></td> <td>maxLength=20</td> <td>Id of the mapping used, as text (not a foreign key, max 20), written with runId. Replay needs it together with recordKey: an ERROR row without mapping or key is not replayable, and a mapping id that no longer exists gives a SKIPPED 'mapping <id> no longer exists' result.</td> </tr> </tbody> </table></div> <h2 id="6-known-gaps-and-documentation-discrepancies">6. Known gaps and documentation discrepancies</h2> <p>Points where the handover guides and the code disagree, or where a feature is incomplete or has not been run end to end.</p> <h3 id="61-connectors-engine">6.1 Connectors engine</h3> <h4 id="611-documentation-versus-code">6.1.1 Documentation versus code</h4> <ol> <li><strong>Record-level <code>${param.name}</code></strong> — GUIDE §5 describes <code>${param.max}</code> as "a parameter passed at run time (workflow, API)". In the engine, record synchronisations resolve inputs with params = null, so <code>${param.*}</code> always yields ""; only feed imports (<code>importFrom</code>) receive parameters. (<code>ConnectorService.prepare</code>)</li> <li><strong>SKIPPED "no applicable mapping"</strong> — GUIDE §13 says SKIPPED covers "no applicable mapping". The engine returns <code>ERROR</code> (<code>No <DIR> mapping for ...</code>) for a missing mapping; SKIPPED is only for blank required inputs, unmatched feed items with creation off, and replays of missing mappings/records. (<code>ConnectorService.syncRecord</code>)</li> <li><strong>Provenance "every write by a connector ... whatever the rule policy"</strong> — GUIDE §4.4. Code records provenance only for main-record fields (paths starting <code>./</code>), not for sub-object rows, not for trigger-driven syncs (<code>syncRecordInProcedure</code>), and not when the <code>FieldProvenance</code> table is absent. (<code>ConnectorRepository.recordProvenance</code>)</li> <li><strong>Conflicts in table triggers</strong> — GUIDE/README present TRUSTED conflicts as always logged; <code>syncRecordInProcedure</code> never calls <code>logConflicts</code>.</li> <li><strong>OAuth token renewal "a minute before they expire"</strong> — GUIDE §3.1.1 and the class comment. Code uses skew = min(60 s, max(1 s, expires_in/10)), i.e. less than a minute for tokens shorter than 10 minutes. (<code>OAuth2.request</code>)</li> <li><strong>HttpJsonClient comment "Retries once on HTTP 429 after a short pause"</strong> — actual policy retries once on 408, 425, 429 and 500–504, only for idempotent methods (never POST/PATCH), honouring <code>Retry-After</code>, 2 s base delay. (<code>HttpRetryPolicy</code>)</li> <li><strong>Secret field length</strong> — <code>CredentialGuide.SECRET_MAX_LENGTH = 255</code> and GUIDE §3.1 / README ("255 characters of the field") vs <code>Connectors.xsd</code>: <code>password</code> and <code>apiKey</code> have <code>maxLength = 4000</code>.</li> <li><strong>Number of seeded connectors</strong> — GUIDE §2 says 40 seeded connectors; README says "about 60"; the installer helpers create at least 61 distinct ids.</li> <li><strong>Installer class comment</strong> — says it seeds "GLEIF, Sirene and Google Geocoding connectors plus the mappings for /root/Companies"; the installer now runs a dozen seed groups.</li> <li><strong><code>logOfRun</code> "most recent first"</strong> — the Javadoc says so, but no sort criteria are set. (<code>ConnectorRepository.logOfRun</code>)</li> <li><strong>GUIDE §7 <code>regex</code> "group default 1"</strong> — default is 1 only when the pattern has capturing groups, else 0. <strong><code>boolean</code></strong> — GUIDE lists true values; code also accepts <code>y</code> and <code>o</code> and returns <code>false</code> (not null) for any other non-blank text.</li> <li><strong>GUIDE §2 table list</strong> — lists 7 tables; the engine also uses <code>FieldProvenance</code>, <code>Conflict</code>, <code>OutboundState</code>, <code>SynchronizationCheckpoint</code>, <code>ConnectorOperationalMetrics</code>, <code>ExecutionRun</code>, <code>SchemaRefreshState</code>, <code>ConnectorCategory</code>, <code>ConnectorTemplate</code>, release tables.</li> <li><strong>GUIDE §4.2 "OK (at least one change), NO_CHANGE, or ERROR"</strong> — omits that rule-level errors do not make the status ERROR (§3.1).</li> <li><strong>Incremental loads</strong> — the model and GUIDE-level fields (<code>loadMode</code>, <code>checkpoint*</code>) suggest incremental synchronisation; the checkpoint manager is not wired into any execution path (§20).</li> <li><strong>Governance and recoverable executions</strong> — <code>ConnectorGovernance</code>, <code>ConnectorGovernanceAudit</code>, <code>ExecutionRun</code> tables and their Java services exist without any production caller (§20).</li> </ol> <h4 id="612-behaviours-worth-documenting-or-fixing-observed-in-code">6.1.2 Behaviours worth documenting or fixing (observed in code)</h4> <ol> <li><strong>Run id leak on pooled threads</strong> — <code>currentRunId()</code> without <code>beginRun()</code> creates an id and stores it in the thread-local without ever removing it; later single calls on the same worker thread reuse that run id, merging unrelated calls into one "run" for replay. (<code>ConnectorService.newRunId</code>)</li> <li><strong>Feeds are invisible to runs and replay</strong> — feed item and header results never get <code>runId</code> or <code>mappingId</code>, so <code>logOfRun</code>/<code>replay</code> cannot see them. (<code>ConnectorService.importItem</code>, <code>importFrom</code>)</li> <li><strong><code>importDocument</code> does not log a missing dataset</strong> (unlike <code>importFrom</code>).</li> <li><strong>Empty key predicate creates rows</strong> — <code>importDocument</code> does not require <code>isFeed()</code>: with a blank <code>keyPredicate</code> every item creates a record (when <code>createIfMissing</code> is true).</li> <li><strong><code>deleteMissing</code> with an absent collection deletes all linked child rows.</strong> (<code>MappingEngine.applyChild</code>)</li> <li><strong>Mappings cannot clear values</strong> — empty or null computed values are never written, whatever the policy.</li> <li><strong>Unbounded deletions</strong> — CHANGED and DATASPACE_DELTA deletions are not limited by <code>max</code>; FAILED and DATASPACE_DELTA ignore the request/predicate passed by the caller.</li> <li><strong>Exhausted retries reported as "waiting for the retry delay"</strong> — records whose attempts reached <code>maxAttempts</code> are counted in <code>waitingRetry</code> with the same message. (<code>OutboundSelector</code>)</li> <li><strong>Re-created records after a deletion</strong> — a <code>DELETED</code> state keeps <code>lastSentAt</code> and the old hash, so under CHANGED a re-created record with identical content is counted as unchanged and never re-sent; under UNSENT it is also "unchanged".</li> <li><strong><code>lastDocument</code> truncation</strong> — documents longer than 8000 characters are stored truncated; their JSON cannot be parsed back for deletions (fields silently missing). Record keys longer than 255 characters are stored truncated while lookups use the full key, so the state row is never found again (insert conflict, error only in the kernel log).</li> <li><strong>Silent SyncLog loss</strong> — values exceeding the model's limits for <code>connector</code> (40), <code>operation</code> (60), <code>origin</code> (60), <code>runId</code> (40) are not truncated; the insert fails inside a swallowed procedure result.</li> <li><strong>Provenance keyed by dataspace</strong> — values written in a workflow child dataspace have no provenance once read in the parent after merge, so they count as manual (trust 100) and block TRUSTED updates.</li> <li><strong>Conflict dedupe ignores dataspace</strong> — an OPEN conflict in one dataspace suppresses the same proposal in another.</li> <li><strong><code>exclude.connectors</code> is case-sensitive in effect</strong> — configured ids are lower-cased but compared with the connector id as stored, so ids containing upper-case letters are never excluded. (<code>OpenLineageEmitter.Settings</code>)</li> <li><strong>Non-atomic conflict acceptance</strong> — the record write and the conflict closure are separate transactions; the conflict lock is JVM-local (the code comment points to a multi-node gap, the security review of September 2026 finding 11).</li> <li><strong>Insecure defaults acknowledged in code</strong> — clear-text secrets allowed and egress unrestricted by default, each with a one-time kernel warning (the security review of September 2026 finding 07).</li> <li><strong>Pipeline splitter ignores quotes</strong> — <code>Transformations.splitPipeline</code> splits on <code>|</code> inside quotes (outside parentheses), while <code>Expressions.firstPipe</code> honours quotes; <code>|></code> detection uses the last occurrence in the whole template.</li> <li><strong><code>lookup</code> always quotes the compared value</strong> — numeric or boolean fields are compared with a string literal.</li> <li><strong>Child key predicates on date fields</strong> use <code>=</code>, which EBX refuses; the explanatory hint exists only for feed key predicates.</li> <li><strong>Global serialisation of connector lookups</strong> — <code>ConnectorRegistry.connector</code> is <code>synchronized</code> and re-reads the configuration (including vault resolution on cache expiry) inside the lock, serialising connector acquisition across all concurrent synchronisations.</li> <li><strong>Workflow aggregate status</strong> — the <code>sendScope</code> summary row (NO_CHANGE) counts as <code>ok</code>, and runs made only of SKIPPED results report <code>NO_DATA</code>. (<code>ebx/SyncTargets</code>, <code>ConnectorScriptTaskBean</code>)</li> <li>No <code>TODO</code>/<code>FIXME</code> markers exist in <code>core</code>.</li> </ol> <h3 id="62-connector-types-and-extension-spi">6.2 Connector types and extension SPI</h3> <h4 id="621-guide-readme-versus-code">6.2.1 GUIDE / README versus code</h4> <ol> <li><strong>Optional libraries (GUIDE §17, §18).</strong> GUIDE says types whose library is absent (Kafka, SFTP, AMQP/JMS providers, JDBC drivers, Azure Service Bus SDK) "appear only when that library is present" / are "simply not registered". Only <code>kafka</code> and <code>sftp</code> are registered quietly; <code>rabbitmq</code>, <code>jms</code>, <code>activemq</code>, <code>azure-service-bus</code> and all JDBC types are always listed and fail at execution.</li> <li><strong>Where jars go (GUIDE §18 vs runtime-libs/README vs root README).</strong> GUIDE §18 tells to put jars in Tomcat's shared <code>lib</code> or <code>WEB-INF/lib</code>; <code>runtime-libs/README.md</code> says the WAR packages them and a second copy in the shared Tomcat library directory must not exist; the root README says <code>kafka-clients</code>, <code>jsch</code> and JDBC drivers come from the EBX installation and are not part of the repository — yet they are in <code>runtime-libs/</code>. GUIDE §18 versions (kafka-clients 3.6.1, jsch 0.2.16) are not stated anywhere in the packaged jars.</li> <li><strong>"JDBC mode" (GUIDE §18).</strong> <code>snowflake</code>, <code>databricks</code> and <code>bigquery</code> have no JDBC mode: they are pure REST connectors; no driver is used.</li> <li><strong>§17 catalogue incomplete versus code.</strong> Missing operations: <code>jdbc</code>/dedicated DB types <code>catalog</code>, <code>profile</code>, <code>sample</code>; <code>snowflake</code>/<code>databricks</code> <code>catalog</code>, <code>profile</code>, <code>sample</code>, <code>usage</code>, <code>lineage</code>; <code>ebx</code> <code>catalog</code>, <code>profile</code>, <code>sample</code>, <code>lineage</code> (documented only in §3.8); <code>kafka</code> <code>catalog</code>, <code>profile</code>, <code>sample</code> (only in §3.9). <code>dynamics-business-central</code> is listed with <code>upsert (W)</code> but the class declares no <code>upsert</code>. Labels differ: code <code>Microsoft SharePoint</code> vs GUIDE "Microsoft SharePoint Online (Microsoft Graph)"; code <code>Oracle NetSuite</code> vs "Oracle NetSuite (SuiteTalk REST/SuiteQL)"; <code>llm</code> label. The <code>rest</code> row lists no operations (they are instance-defined).</li> <li><strong><code>rest</code> operations (GUIDE §3.2).</strong> GUIDE lists <code>GET | POST | PUT | DELETE</code> and "GET/DELETE = read, POST/PUT = write"; the code also accepts <code>PATCH</code> (write), an explicit <code>op.<name>.kind</code>, SEARCH inference from <code>op.<name>.fields</code>, headers, cookies, multipart, pagination and <code>responseRoot</code> — none documented in §3.2. Conversely the descriptor makes <code>op.<name>.method</code> <strong>required</strong> although the code defaults it to <code>GET</code>: an operation declared with only <code>op.x.path</code> fails validation ("op.x.method: A value is required"), and <code>op.x.kind</code> in lower case fails the enumeration check although the code upper-cases it.</li> <li><strong>Secret field length (GUIDE §3.1).</strong> GUIDE says the credential guide warns above "the 255 characters of the field"; the XSD declares <code>password</code> and <code>apiKey</code> with <code>maxLength 4000</code>.</li> <li><strong>OAuth 2.0 "for any REST-based type" (GUIDE §3.1.1).</strong> Many REST types replace <code>headers()</code> and ignore <code>authType OAUTH2</code>: creditsafe, dnb, dynamics365 (own token), jde-ais, opensanctions, databricks, snowflake, bigquery; salesforce and workday overwrite <code>Authorization</code> with their own tokens.</li> <li><strong>Webhook endpoint.</strong> <code>WebhookConnector</code> javadoc cites <code>/ebx-ps-fasttrack/connectors/webhook/{mapping}</code>; GUIDE §12.2 uses <code>/ebx-connectors/rest/v1/webhook/{feed}</code> (javadoc is stale).</li> <li><strong>Extension sample.</strong> Sample README and <code>build.sh</code> expect <code>ebx-connectors-extension-sample/lib/ebx-connectors-spi.jar</code>; the <code>lib/</code> folder is empty in the repository — the jar is in <code>ebx-connectors/dist/</code> (as GUIDE §3.3 says). <code>build.sh</code> without an argument therefore exits 2. The SPI jar is dated 2026.09.11 and may lag later changes to the helpers it embeds.</li> <li><strong>Inventory JSON.</strong> <code>docs/connector-metadata-inventory.json</code> (schemaVersion 1, 40 entries, "audit inventory, not a runtime descriptor contract") is read by no Java class or build script; it lacks 18 registered types (<code>powerbi</code>, <code>collibra</code>, <code>openlineage</code>, <code>postgresql</code>, <code>h2</code>, <code>mysql</code>, <code>oracle-database</code>, <code>sql-server</code>, <code>azure-sql</code>, <code>rabbitmq</code>, <code>jms</code>, <code>activemq</code>, <code>azure-service-bus</code>, <code>sharepoint</code>, <code>netsuite</code>, <code>dynamics-business-central</code>, <code>sap-successfactors</code>, <code>sap-ariba</code>) and is stale on others: <code>llm</code> lists only anthropic/openai/azure; <code>ebx</code>, <code>kafka</code>, <code>jdbc</code>, <code>snowflake</code>, <code>databricks</code> lack their catalog operations; <code>dnb</code> lacks monitoring operations; <code>soap</code> says WSDL discovery is "not evident" (it is implemented); <code>kafka</code> auth omits SCRAM; <code>jdbc</code> lacks <code>jndi</code>/<code>fetch.size</code>/<code>batch.size</code>; categories follow the old <code>defaultCategory</code> (e.g. <code>jdbc</code> DATA_PLATFORMS vs installer fallback DATABASE).</li> <li><strong>Categories.</strong> <code>jdbc</code> descriptor says <code>DATA_PLATFORMS</code>, the installer's legacy fallback says <code>DATABASE</code>; <code>openlineage</code> descriptor <code>DATA_PLATFORMS</code>, not listed in the legacy fallback (would be GENERIC_INTEGRATION).</li> <li><strong>XSD documentation.</strong> <code>Connector/active</code> description reads "An inactive mapping is never selected…" (copied from the Mapping table).</li> </ol> <h4 id="622-code-level-inconsistencies-and-limitations">6.2.2 Code-level inconsistencies and limitations</h4> <ol start="13"> <li><strong>Validator requires <code>baseUrl</code> where the code defaults it</strong>: <code>bigquery</code>, <code>gcs</code>, <code>s3</code> (derived URL), <code>powerbi</code> (DEFAULT_BASE_URL), <code>netsuite</code> (from <code>account</code>). A blank <code>baseUrl</code> makes the connector unusable ("baseUrl: A base URL is required").</li> <li><strong>JNDI without <code>baseUrl</code></strong>: only <code>postgresql</code> removes the generic <code>baseUrl</code> issues when <code>jndi</code> is set; <code>jdbc</code>, <code>h2</code>, <code>mysql</code>, <code>oracle-database</code>, <code>sql-server</code>, <code>azure-sql</code> return the default result early and still fail with "A base URL is required" when <code>baseUrl</code> is blank.</li> <li><strong>Validation before <code>configure</code></strong>: type defaults written in <code>configure</code> (drivers, <code>jdbc.encrypt</code>, <code>auth.mode</code>, Ariba paths, JMS factory…) are not seen by validation; harmless for the current rules, but the declared "required" properties with code defaults (e.g. Business Central <code>environment</code>, whose descriptor default <code>Production</code> is ignored by its own validator) must be entered explicitly.</li> <li><strong>Declared types versus parsing</strong>: <code>loqate</code> <code>verification.min</code> declared INTEGER but the code compares with <code>V</code> (a numeric value disables filtering; <code>V</code> fails the INTEGER check, so the default filter can only be the implicit one). <code>ban</code> <code>score.min</code>, <code>dnb</code> <code>confidence.min</code>, <code>opensanctions</code> <code>threshold</code>, <code>llm</code> <code>temperature</code> declared STRING (no numeric validation; invalid numbers throw <code>NumberFormatException</code> at run time). <code>dnb</code> <code>tradeUp</code> declared BOOLEAN but its documented value is <code>hq</code>, which fails the BOOLEAN check. <code>llm</code> <code>maxTokens</code> parsed with <code>Integer.parseInt</code> without fallback.</li> <li><strong>Unchecked number parsing of inputs</strong> (<code>NumberFormatException</code> instead of a <code>ConnectorException</code>): <code>orbis</code> <code>max</code>, <code>hubspot</code> <code>max</code>, <code>bigquery</code> <code>max</code>, <code>AbstractFileConnector</code> <code>max</code> and property <code>max.rows</code>.</li> <li><strong><code>graphql</code> and <code>soap</code> before configuration</strong>: their <code>getOperations()</code> call <code>getConfig()</code>, which throws <code>IllegalStateException</code> on an unconfigured instance (the <code>config == null</code> checks are dead code); <code>getDescriptor()</code> catches it, other callers do not. <code>rest</code> handles it.</li> <li><strong>Kafka <code>peek</code></strong> is described as "the latest messages without committing", but it subscribes with group <code><group.id>-peek</code> and <code>auto.offset.reset=earliest</code>, never committing: it always returns the <strong>oldest</strong> retained messages. <code>consume</code> commits offsets of non-JSON messages it did not return (they are skipped permanently, reported only in <code>invalidMessages</code>). The <code>envelope.table</code> value is the string <code>"null"</code> when the input is absent.</li> <li><strong>Snowflake asynchronous statements</strong>: when a statement is still running (HTTP 202) at the readTimeoutMs deadline, the 202 body is treated as a success and an empty result (<code>count</code> 0, <code>message</code>) is returned instead of an error.</li> <li><strong>Object-store reads are text-based</strong>: <code>s3</code>, <code>azure-blob</code>, <code>gcs</code> convert the response body to a string then to UTF-8 bytes; binary objects are corrupted (irrelevant for CSV/JSON/XML, relevant for a future Excel/Parquet codec). Pagination descriptors (continuation tokens, <code>NextMarker</code>, <code>nextPageToken</code>, BigQuery <code>pageToken</code>) are advertised but not followed; <code>list</code> returns at most one page (≤1000, ≤5000 for Azure).</li> <li><strong>SFTP host keys</strong>: without <code>knownHosts</code>, <code>StrictHostKeyChecking=no</code> (no verification) whatever <code>strictHostKey</code> says.</li> <li><strong>Azure Service Bus settlement</strong> depends on an in-memory static lock table: <code>complete</code>/<code>abandon</code> work only in the same JVM within 5 minutes of <code>receive</code>; the input <code>entity</code> is always required even though an <code>entity</code> property is declared.</li> <li><strong>JDBC <code>query</code> safety filter</strong> rejects any SELECT containing the word <code>into</code> (even in a literal or identifier) or a <code>;</code>; <code>profile.where</code> is concatenated verbatim into SQL; <code>DriverManager.setLoginTimeout</code> is JVM-global.</li> <li><strong>BigQuery <code>upsert</code></strong> builds a MERGE with inline literals rather than parameters.</li> <li><strong>Collibra PATCH</strong> assumes <code>java.net.http</code> (JDK 11+); on Java 8 PATCH would not be converted to the <code>_HttpMethod</code> override.</li> <li><strong>Unused/stub members</strong>: <code>HubSpotConnector.unused()</code> returns null; <code>GleifConnector.firstAsRecord</code> builds an unused <code>keep</code> list; <code>DnbConnector.describeFields</code> has a no-op <code>prefix</code>; <code>SapODataConnector.collection</code>, <code>normalise</code>, <code>parseMetadata</code> are unused wrappers kept for compatibility; <code>SapAribaConnector</code> declares <code>api.version</code> but never uses it; <code>SynchronizationStrategy</code> and most <code>ConnectorCapability</code> / <code>PaginationDescriptor</code> values are metadata only.</li> <li><strong>Descriptors inconsistent with behaviour</strong>: generic <code>jdbc</code> advertises no <code>TEST_CONNECTION</code> though it implements it; <code>webhook</code> advertises PREVIEW_RECORDS but has no preview; <code>s3</code> advertises API_KEY_HEADER although it uses access keys in username/password; <code>bigquery</code> advertises <code>dataset</code> as required although fully qualified table names work without it; <code>workday</code> requires <code>clientId</code>/<code>refreshToken</code> even for RaaS-only use.</li> <li><strong>Tested offline only</strong> (GUIDE statements, not verifiable in code): <code>powerbi</code> and <code>collibra</code> against fakes; LLM providers Gemini/Mistral/Azure never called against a real service (per project memory).</li> </ol> <h3 id="63-on-ebx-integration-services-ui-rest-mcp-automation">6.3 ON EBX integration: services, UI, REST, MCP, automation</h3> <p><strong>Documentation vs code</strong> 1. <strong>Webhook path in the seeded description.</strong> The <code>Webhook</code> connector's description says <code>POST /ebx-ps-fasttrack/connectors/webhook/{mapping id or label}</code>. The real endpoint is <code>POST /ebx-connectors/rest/v1/webhook/{feed}</code>. The <code>JDBC</code> description likewise points drivers to <code>ebx-ps-fasttrack-server/lib</code>, whereas drivers are packaged in the module WAR (<code>runtime-libs</code>). 2. <strong>Connector count.</strong> GUIDE §2 says "40 seeded"; the installer seeds <strong>61</strong> connectors. The GUIDE §2 table lists 7 tables; the model has 22 (templates, releases, provenance, conflicts, outbound states, checkpoints, metrics, governance, execution runs, schema refresh). 3. <strong>Where the sync service is offered.</strong> GUIDE §2 says it appears on "every table that has at least one mapping". The declaration activates on all tables of all datasets; tables without mappings show a hint. GUIDE §12.1 lists 3 scopes; the service offers 7 (plus UNSENT, CHANGED, FAILED, DATASPACE_DELTA). 4. <strong>Declared input parameters of the sync service.</strong> GUIDE §12.1 says <code>mapping</code>, <code>inputs</code> and <code>dryRun</code> are "declared as input parameters". Only <code>mapping</code> is declared (<code>addInputParameter</code>); the bundle keys <code>sync.param.inputs*</code> / <code>sync.param.dryRun*</code> exist but are unused. <code>inputs</code> and <code>dryRun</code> are still read from the session or request. 5. <strong>Workflow bean <code>inputs</code>.</strong> GUIDE §12.3 lists <code>inputs</code> as a bean input. The bean has the setter, but <code>module.xml</code> does not declare the property, so the library editor does not show it. GUIDE also mentions a ready-made model <code>ConnectorEnrichment</code> that is not in <code>tools/export/workflows/</code>. 6. <strong>MCP <code>connectors_sync.inputs</code>.</strong> Catalogued and handled by <code>ConnectorsTools</code>, but absent from the native <code>ConnectorsMcpServer.sync</code> signature, so it is unreachable through <code>/ebx-assistant/mcp/v1/ebx-connectors</code>. Its catalogue description says "JSON object text, e.g. {"maxMessages":"50"}", but the value is parsed as <code>name=value;…</code> by <code>ConnectorService.runtimeInputs</code>, so JSON text would not be understood as documented. 7. <strong>Parameter type mismatches.</strong> <code>connectors_config_import.config</code> is typed "JSON text" (string) in the catalogue and <code>Object</code> in the native server. <code>connectors_mapping_delete.mapping</code> is documented as "Mapping id" but labels are resolved too. 8. <strong>Record key parameter name.</strong> REST <code>/conflicts</code> uses <code>key</code>; MCP and the conflict manager use <code>recordKey</code>. The <code>agentInstruction</code> of <code>DemoEnrichmentConflicts.json</code> tells agents to call <code>GET /conflicts?recordKey=<id></code>, which REST ignores (the list is then not narrowed). 9. <strong>Log origins.</strong> GUIDE §13 lists <code>user service</code>, <code>workflow admin</code>, <code>REST admin</code>. The code writes <code>userService</code>, <code>workflow <user></code>, <code>REST <user></code>, <code>MCP <user></code>, <code>scheduler</code>, <code>designer</code>, <code>webhook <connectorId></code>, <code>replay <runId> by <user></code>. 10. <strong>Webhook authentication.</strong> Settled by C2-10: one mode, an EBX session with the RUN right. The shared-secret and HMAC-signature modes were removed (GUIDE §12.2). 11. <strong>Script paths.</strong> GUIDE §15 refers to <code>connectors-module/import_config.py</code> and <code>connectors-module/config_gleif_wikidata.py</code>; they live in <code>tools/</code>. <code>build_xsd.py</code>'s header also says "Generated by connectors-module/build_xsd.py". 12. <strong>XSD generation.</strong> <code>tools/README.md</code> says "edit the description, never the XSD", but <code>build_xsd.py</code> knows only 9 tables and lacks <code>ConnectorCategory</code>, <code>category</code>, <code>uploadedLogo</code>, <code>logoUrl</code> placement, <code>connection*</code> fields, <code>ConnectorTemplate</code>, release tables, <code>SchemaRefreshState</code>, <code>SynchronizationCheckpoint</code>, <code>loadMode</code>/checkpoint fields, metrics, governance, <code>ExecutionRun</code> and the metrics record form. Regenerating would drop them: the deployed XSD has been edited by hand. 13. <strong><code>extend_xsd.py</code> is misfiled.</strong> It patches a Companies model at a hard-coded absolute path of a FastTrack workspace; it is not a Connectors tool. 14. <strong>Stale code comments.</strong> <code>MappingDesignerServiceDeclaration</code> says the host module name contains spaces (the former FastTrack module name); the module is now <code>ebx-connectors</code>. <code>ConnectorSyncServiceDeclaration</code>'s comment says "the service itself decides whether a mapping exists": in practice it is always shown and only displays a hint when none exists. 15. <strong>Credential field length.</strong> The BEARER warning and the page meter use a 255-character limit (<code>CredentialGuide.SECRET_MAX_LENGTH</code>), while <code>password</code>/<code>apiKey</code> are declared with <code>maxLength</code> 4000 in the XSD. Pasted secrets between 256 and 4000 characters are flagged "Too long for this field" although EBX accepts them. 16. <strong>Connection status enumeration.</strong> The XSD enumerates NOT_CONNECTED|CONNECTED|FAILED; the UI also shows STALE, computed at read time (consistent, but undocumented in the model). 17. <strong>Release notes status.</strong> <code>release-notes.xml</code> <code>status</code> attributes (<code>in-progress</code>, <code>complete</code>) are ignored: every development release is stored <code>IN_PROGRESS</code> and formal ones <code>CURRENT</code>. The labels <code>releaseNotes.status.IN_PROGRESS</code> / <code>DRAFT</code> have no bundle entry, so they render as the raw text <code>IN PROGRESS</code>. 18. <strong>Log message.</strong> <code>seedLot2</code> logs "seeded Claude, EORI, Countries, EBX, ChatWebhook, INPI and OpenFDA" but also creates JMS and ActiveMQ. 19. <strong>Seed guard with the <code>never</code> policy.</strong> <code>seedCatalog</code> and friends still create connectors (by design), but <code>childMapping</code> never checks <code>never</code> directly (it relies on the parent being skipped).</p> <p><strong>Unfinished or unused in code</strong> 20. <strong>Unused persistence adapters.</strong> <code>EbxSynchronizationCheckpointStore</code>, <code>EbxRecoverableExecutionStore</code> and <code>EbxConnectorGovernanceStore</code> have no caller in <code>main</code> (tests only). The governance API (<code>docs/GOVERNANCE.md</code>: READ/MANAGE/APPROVE/AUDIT, actor from the session) has no REST, MCP or UI adapter in this layer. The bundle keys <code>governance.*</code> are unused. 21. <strong>Dead code.</strong> <code>ConnectorsInstaller.needsSeed()</code> and <code>tables()</code> are unused. <code>ConnectorSyncUserService.hasMapping()</code> is unused. <code>designer.catalogLoaded</code> is an unused bundle key. 22. <strong>Mapping form coverage.</strong> <code>MappingForm</code> does not render <code>deleteOperation</code>, <code>maxAttempts</code>, <code>retryDelayMinutes</code>. Its Synchronization tab hint is hard-coded (English/French) instead of coming from the bundle. 23. <strong>Delete preview count.</strong> <code>DeleteMappingUserService</code>'s preview (<code>attachments</code>) omits <code>SynchronizationCheckpoint</code> rows, which <code>cascade</code> deletes. 24. <strong>Runs without <code>beginRun</code>.</strong> In <code>ConnectorSyncUserService</code>, the SELECTED/VIEW/ALL and feed paths call <code>syncRecords</code>/<code>importFrom</code> without <code>beginRun</code>/<code>endRun</code>; only the outbound-scope path gets a run id and the "replay" message. The workflow bean calls <code>endRun</code> outside a <code>finally</code>. 25. <strong>Loop order.</strong> The workflow bean on a table iterates mapping-first, not record-first as its comment states. 26. <strong>Import and lineage.</strong> <code>ConfigTransfer.importConfig</code> disables triggers and schedules no declared lineage, unlike <code>upsertMapping</code> and <code>deleteMapping</code>; run <code>POST /lineage/declare</code> afterwards. 27. <strong><code>GET /webhook/{feed}</code> exposure.</strong> It does not call <code>ensure</code> or check the session explicitly. Whether anonymous callers reach it depends on the REST Toolkit's authentication. 28. <strong>Diagnostics connect action.</strong> The diagnostics service offers no <strong>Connect</strong> button; connect is only reachable through the connector form (<code>ftcdAction=connect</code>). 29. <strong>Admin role coverage.</strong> <code>ebx.connectors.adminRole</code> protects 7 configuration tables only; <code>Conflict</code>, <code>ConnectorTemplate</code>, release, governance and runtime tables stay under plain EBX permissions. 30. <strong>Open security findings.</strong> the security review of September 2026 marks 07 (egress allow-list is not default-deny; plaintext secrets not rejected; redirects not validated) and 11 (conflict acceptance is not atomic across the record and connector transactions) as <strong>still open</strong>. The release notes list the previous-schema upgrade and fresh-install acceptance (pass 2), the Excel provider (pass 6), and PostgreSQL governed-run acceptance (pass 9) as pending.</p></main> </div>
